Witness: `Parallax_Update` (parallax.emp:345, `preserves(sr.mask)`) has a mid-body `jbra Parallax_Step5_Vscroll` (parallax.emp:534, on a conditional path) whose target does not declare `preserves(sr.mask)`; the mask survives that path IN FACT (the body restored SR before the branch and Step5 writes no SR) but it is UNPROVEN — the terminal-only slice never looks. **Scope (2026-08-06 sr.mask-plumbing census): closing this is NOT a cheap extension of `terminal_external_tail` to every `Defer` — it IS the S2-D7 path-sensitive scope in disguise.** The register walk already charges every `Defer` because it runs a full dataflow (`verify_preserved` over all edges); the mask slice inspects only the last instruction precisely because it has NO dataflow (static-order + terminal). Unifying the mask onto the every-`Defer` discipline means giving the mask claim the same per-path save/restore dataflow the register walk has — i.e. BUILDING the S2-D7 mask half, not widening the terminal probe. A `terminal_external_tail`-style widening to mid-body tails would credit the leave without proving SR was restored on that path, re-opening the vacuity the terminal slice closes. Pre-existing (the mask slice was always static-order + terminal), out of the terminal-credit parcel's scope. — OPEN (terminal-only scope; kill: the S2-D7 mask dataflow half — the mid-body mask credit IS that half, not a `terminal_external_tail` widening).
- [preserves-through-tail, 2026-08-06] **`collect_sr_mask_preservers` reads DECLARED `preserves` only, not the module-`invariant` union the design's oracle names.** A 68k proc that preserves the mask solely through an inherited module-scope `invariant: preserves(sr.mask)` would be silently uncredited by the tail credit (a refusal — the safe direction). Corpus-dead: `invariant` is a rung-2 Z80 construct with no 68k adopter today. — **STILL OPEN, and the naive union is RULED AGAINST 2026-08-06 (sr.mask plumbing lane: the union was built, caught by the B/C lens panel, and REVERTED in full).** What the lane established, so the next porter does not rebuild it:

  1. **The credit is a PROOF DISCHARGE, not a hint.** `sr_mask_preservers_credit` returning true suppresses the ERROR-tier `[proc.preserves-sr-unbalanced]` obligation and RETURNS EARLY, skipping even `sr_writes_round_trip` on the claimant. Anything that adds a member adds an unexamined error-suppression.
  2. **On 68k nothing enforces a module invariant against any body.** The Z80 arm forces every proc to prove it (`check.extend(invariant_regs)` in `check_z80_preserves`); the 68k arm never consults `invariant_regs` at all. So the union credits procs that no checker examines.
  3. **The Proc arm is an EPISTEMIC REGRESSION, not an extension.** Today a proc enters the map only by declaring `preserves(sr)`/`preserves(sr.mask)` — which on 68k is non-empty and therefore DOES run `check_preserves` → `check_preserves_sr` on that body. The union replaced verified-then-trusted with trusted-outright: the wrong direction.
  4. **The corpus surface is not protected by the accident that neutralises this in `lower`.** `analyze_corpus_with_contracts` never calls `validate_module_invariants`, so the `[contract.unknown-register]` rejection does not fire there — and that surface is gated by the warn_tier / contract_closure / dead_save corpus tests. A module invariant nobody checks could soften real gate verdicts.
  5. **The `extern proc` arm is ruled AGAINST outright.** It credits a FOREIGN symbol on the strength of a promise made by a module that does not own it — directly contradicting `collect_z80_callee_preserves`, which documents the opposite rule ("the invariant is a property of THIS module's procs, not the extern's").
  6. **`validate_module_invariants` cannot even ACCEPT the construct on 68k today.** `invariant_reg_name` takes a single-segment path (so `sr.mask` cannot be spelled) and `expand_reglist`/`RegFile::M68k` has no `sr` unit (so `sr` is `[contract.unknown-register]`). BOTH spellings are hard errors — the earlier claim that "the two paths recognize the mask token by its spelling" is FALSE and has been struck from the code comment and this row.

  **The ordering trap, stated explicitly:** SR-family recognition in `validate_module_invariants` is a PRECONDITION for any 68k module invariant — but landing that recognition ALONE takes the credit live with still no enforcement behind it. The correct order is enforcement FIRST (a 68k arm that checks every proc body against the inherited invariant, the way Z80 does), then the credit, gated on check-then-credit. Do not land SR recognition as a standalone "small fix". Building the 68k enforcer is a real parcel, not a rider. — OPEN (corpus-dead; kill: a 68k invariant ENFORCER, after which membership may follow proof — never before).

### B′-2 stack delta (2026-08-04) — what the sp model does not yet see

- [bprime-2, 2026-08-04] **`pea` is not modeled as a stack push.** `pea <ea>` pushes 4 bytes with no `-(sp)` operand, so `preserves::transfer` treats it as a plain instruction and the slot map under-counts sp by 4 from that point. Zero corpus sites today (`grep -rniE '^\s*pea\b' aeon --include='*.emp'` = 0; the single hit is prose in `error_handler.emp:25`). The direction is SAFE — under-counting produces silence or an underflow bail, never a false `[stack.unbalanced]` — but it is a hole the day someone writes one, and it degrades the entry-value proof too (a `pea` between a save and its restore misaligns the slot map). Fix: one arm in `transfer` pushing an opaque 4-byte slot. Cheap; deliberately not taken because a byte-neutral parcel should not add an untestable-by-corpus arm without a demand. — OPEN
- [bprime-2, 2026-08-04] **`rte` shares the `rts` balance rule, and the art-streaming plan describes a shape that would FALSE-POSITIVE.** `flag_check::RETURN_MNEMONICS` groups `rts`/`rte`/`rtr`/`rtd`, and `[stack.unbalanced]` demands depth 0 at all four. That is right for the corpus's one `rte` (`vblank.emp:56`, a balanced `movem.l d0-a6` pair — it is in the 401 clean sites). It is NOT right for a handler that hand-builds its own exception frame: `plans/2026-07-02-art-streaming-phase2.md:192` proposes `move.w (PageIn_Saved_SR).w, -(sp)` / `rte`, which the model would report at depth 2. Likewise `rtd #N` pops N extra bytes (68010+, unreachable on this hardware) and `rtr` pops a CCR word; both are zero-site. The escape exists and is tested (`@allow("stack.unbalanced")`), so this is a documented sharp edge rather than a blocker — but whoever lands art-streaming phase 2 must decide between the `@allow` and an `rte`-aware rule. — OPEN
- [bprime-2, 2026-08-04] **`find_dead_saves` still runs its OWN transfer and join.** `ds_transfer`/`ds_join` at the bottom of `preserves.rs` model the same stack with a STRICTLY MORE CONSERVATIVE bailout set — notably it bails on `addq #4,sp` (any bare `a7` operand trips its `sp_hazard` call) where the shared `transfer` models the immediate cleanup exactly, and its `DsSlot` carries no byte width so `ds_join` compares slot COUNT rather than bytes. Safe for a code-CUTTING worklist, where extra conservatism only loses suggestions. B′-2 extracted `run_stack_dataflow` so the entry-value proof and the balance checker provably share one bailout set; the dead-save analysis is the one place in the module where that claim does not hold. **Lens B caught that the parcel initially made it WORSE in the one direction that matters** — `ds_transfer` neither modeled nor bailed on `link`/`unlk` (neither has an `a7` operand), so after a frame its depth was wrong and a later restore could pair a slot to the wrong register, i.e. a WRONG dead-save suggestion rather than a missing one. Closed in-parcel with an explicit `link`/`unlk` bail. The remaining divergence is all in the suppressing direction. Folding it onto the shared driver would need `DsSlot`'s per-slot clobber/callee accumulation carried as observer state rather than slot state. — OPEN
- [bprime-2 lens C, 2026-08-04] **The checker structurally cannot report sp TOO HIGH.** `Slot::bytes` is unsigned, so the slot map has no negative depth: an sp RAISED past its entry value drains the tracked slots and hits the underflow bail rather than producing a positive delta. `[stack.unbalanced]` therefore covers exactly one of the two imbalance directions, and the "popped more than was pushed" class is a blind spot. Verified by probe: `addq.l #8, sp` after one long push is silent, and `move.w (sp)+, sr` at depth 0 is silent. Not unsound (silence), and the finding now reports a DEPTH rather than a signed delta so nothing in the surface implies the other direction is checked. Closing it wants a signed depth plus a model for what sits below entry, which is the caller's frame and not this proc's business. — OPEN
- [bprime-2 lens C, 2026-08-04] **The `move.l #Target,-(sp)` + `rts` computed jump FIRES.** The `pea Target` spelling of the same idiom is silent (the unmodeled-`pea` under-count, row above), so the two spellings disagree. Zero corpus sites — verified `grep -E '#[A-Za-z_.][A-Za-z0-9_.]*\s*,\s*-\(sp\)'` returns nothing, and the parcel's own `-(sp)` mnemonic histogram accounts for all 248 sites. `@allow("stack.unbalanced")` is the escape if it ever appears; the principled fix is to recognise a push-then-`rts` as a computed transfer, which is also what `pea`+`rts` should be recognised as. — OPEN
- [bprime-2 lens C, 2026-08-04] **`is_safe_sp_disp_read` stays narrow while `is_sp_top_read` was generalised, so the two sp-read exemptions now disagree on mnemonic.** The top-of-stack exemption is mnemonic-agnostic (a load cannot alter a slot, whatever the opcode — the corpus needs it for `adda.w (sp), a2` at `sprites.emp:257`); the DISPLACED read exemption is still `move`/`movea` only. A corpus `adda.w 4(sp), a2` would bail where `adda.w (sp), a2` does not, for no principled reason. Deliberately not widened in-parcel: it is a behaviour change to a shipped ERROR gate with no corpus demand, and widening it would also change which procs the balance census can prove. — OPEN
- [bprime-2 lens B, 2026-08-04] **A named stack frame is the construct the residual bail surface is asking for.** 100% of what the balance checker cannot measure on the corpus is ONE pattern: a hand-rolled frame with read-write `d(sp)` locals. `tile_cache.emp:528` draws its own layout in a comment — `// stack layout: 0(sp)=end_row, 2(sp)=cur_row, 4(sp)=end_col, 6(sp)=start_col` — then indexes it by hand for ~120 lines and tears it down with two `addq.l #4, sp`; `children.emp` does the same with a 2-slot running position. A declared frame (`frame { end_row: u16, cur_row: u16, … }`, B′-1's context machinery generalised from a machine-state lattice to a memory layout) would name the offsets, let the compiler own the epilogue, and — the part that matters here — tell the model the frame's extent, so a write to a NAMED local is provably not aliasing a saved slot. The bail disappears and stack coverage goes to 100%. This is the same "a comment that should have been a checked declaration" shape B′-1's headline celebrated, and the corpus wrote its own demand for it. — OPEN
- [bprime-2, 2026-08-04] **A tail transfer out of a proc is never charged a stack delta**, so `move.l d0,-(sp)` followed by `jmp Foo` is unreported. B′-2 inherits the reason `preserves` already gives for ignoring `Edge::Defer`: the transfer may diverge (a noreturn error rail owes its caller nothing) and nothing in the language marks that. Same `@noreturn` language ask B′-0b's ledger already carries — this is a second consumer for it, which is the argument for building it. — **@noreturn SHIPPED (noreturn-tail lane, 2026-08-05); the stack-delta charge stays OFF — census outcome, recorded.** The noreturn model now marks divergence, so B′-2 CAN tell a divergent tail (`raise_error`/handler `jmp`, now `@noreturn`-markable) from a real tail call. But the charge was NOT enabled — and the census (Lens C re-ran it with line numbers) is stronger than "dominated by locals": the push-then-EXTERNAL-tail set is **EMPTY**. Every corpus push-then-transfer is push + `jbra .local` (intra-proc — `Edge::Follow`, never a `Defer` exit, so never the stack-checker's concern): `section.emp:533/584/628/667` (save-around-call, balanced before a local `jbra`), `sprites.emp:249/:254` (intra-proc loop pushes); `pea` in code = **0**; push-then-external-tail = **0**. So there is no legitimate arg-passing tail to protect AND no site the charge would fire on today — the charge is left OFF per the spec's default because (1) turning it on is a `preserves.rs` `BalanceObserver` Defer-arm change (`charges(ExitKind::Defer) = false`, run_stack_dataflow ~:466-472) that is byte-frozen this lane, and (2) any FUTURE push-then-external-tail must be adjudicated when it appears, with `@noreturn` now available to exclude the divergent ones. Enabling it (charge a `Defer` exit's stack delta when the target is NOT `@noreturn`) is a dedicated preserves.rs parcel with a full corpus run — now UNBLOCKED on the divergence distinction it named here.

### noreturn-tail lane — panel fix-up (2026-08-05)

- [noreturn-tail lens B, 2026-08-05] **THE TAIL-SPELLING DEFECT FAMILY'S NEXT INSTANCE — an unconditional 68k transfer to a TRAILING local label classifies as `Edge::Defer`, not `FallOff`.** `Cfg::edges` (68k) maps a branch target through `label_target`, which only holds labels with a FOLLOWING instruction; a label that CLOSES the body has no entry, so `bra .end` / `jbra .end` where `.end:` is last reads as an external `Defer` — indistinguishable from a real tail transfer, though control runs straight off the end. The Z80 builder already models this (edge-split §5.2 closed the Z80 twin). Two of this lane's new checks would have accepted the lie: `check_noreturn` (a `@noreturn` proc that falls off via `bra .out` passes) and `ccr_bracket_refusal` (a `preserves(sr.ccr)`/bare-`sr` proc whose `jbra .end` reads as intra-proc flow, ACCEPTED where it was refused). **RULED FIX (narrow): both consumers resolve the shape themselves** — `check_noreturn` treats a `Defer` to a trailing local label as a `FallOff`; the CCR walk gates intra-proc on `cfg.label_index(t).is_some()` (None for a trailing label) instead of `is_local_label`. **The Cfg::edges UNIFICATION (make the 68k builder return `FallOff` for a trailing local, like Z80) is deliberately NOT taken here** — it changes classification for EVERY 68k `Cfg` consumer (flag_check, out_verify, context, preserves, cycle_budget, z80_bus's 68k table), a cross-analysis blast radius that wants its own parcel with a per-consumer census. Both counterexamples pinned verbatim (`noreturn_trailing_local_transfer_is_a_fall_off`, `ccr_trailing_local_transfer_is_a_leave`). — **CLOSED** by the cfg-edges-unification parcel (2026-08-06): the shared `Cfg::branch_edge` three-way (`label_target` hit → `Follow`; miss but `is_local_label` → `FallOff`; else `Defer`) now classifies a trailing local as `FallOff` on BOTH CPUs, with both 68k arms (unconditional + conditional-taken) and both Z80 `jp`/`jr` sites routed through it, and `check_noreturn`'s narrow `is_trailing_local` shim retired into the shared `FallOff` arm (its two pins survive verbatim, asserting the `[noreturn.returns]`/`[proc.preserves-unverifiable]` tags). The unification has NO corpus positive witness today: both file-level 68k trailing labels (`buffers.emp:57 jbra .done`, `frames.emp:67 jbra .rpc_done`) are comptime-fn template bodies (`queue_static_dma`, `refresh_piece_count`) that always inline MID-proc, so every closing label acquires a following instruction and resolves to `Follow` — the census (contract report + warn-tier, all seven shapes) is byte-for-byte unchanged, and `Process_DMA_Critical`'s `@budget 670` is unmoved. The first NON-inlined trailing-label proc to enter the corpus will be the first live witness of the generalized behavior. (`ccr_bracket_refusal` KEPT: it reads `label_index` directly, not `edges()`, so `branch_edge` cannot serve it — a reader-level gate keyed on the SAME map, cannot drift from the builder; recorded here, not a defect.)
- [noreturn-tail lens B, 2026-08-05] **A data-only `@noreturn` body passes `[noreturn.returns]` VACUOUSLY.** The check walks instruction edges; a body with no `CodeItem::Instr` (only `CodeItem::Inline` dc.l data — `ErrorHandlerBlob`) has no edges, so nothing to refuse. This is an ACCEPTED-LIE edge for a literally-empty proc: the claim is trusted, not proven, when there is no code to prove it against. Not a defect to "fix" — `ErrorHandlerBlob` is load-bearing (the vendored MD-Debugger blob whose internal `rts`/`jmp` encodings live inside the data, unreachable by the CFG) and its divergence IS a trust root, exactly like the transitive claim. Recorded so the edge is named, not silently relied on. — RECORDED (accepted-lie edge, by design).
- [noreturn-tail lens B, 2026-08-05] **`jsr`/`bsr`/`call` to a `@noreturn` target is still `[cycles.opaque-call]` in cycle_budget.** The divergent-terminal close fires only for a TRANSFER edge (`jbra`/`jmp`/`bra`, or an authored rail's `jmp`); a CALL to a `@noreturn` target is refused as an opaque call before edges are consulted. Conservative and correct for a budget (a call's own return path is the callee's, and a `@noreturn` callee that a caller `jsr`s rather than tail-jumps is an unusual shape), but recorded: a `jsr Diverge` where `Diverge` is `@noreturn` could in principle close too. Zero corpus consumers (no `@budget` proc), so not taken. — RECORDED (conservative; no consumer).
- [noreturn-tail lens B, 2026-08-05] **The `@noreturn` set is PER-MODULE.** `collect_noreturn_symbols` walks one file's items; a divergent tail whose target is `@noreturn` in ANOTHER module is not seen unless that module's contract is re-declared locally via `extern proc @noreturn`. This matches every other per-module contract map (`CalleePreserves`, the resolver's symbol scope) and the adoption needs no cross-module case (each adopter's divergence is consumed within its own module). Recorded so a future cross-module divergent-tail consumer knows the extern redeclaration is the seam. — RECORDED (per-module, extern redecl is the seam).
- [bprime-2, 2026-08-04] **Two corpus procs cannot be proven stack-balanced, both for the sp-STORE bailout.** `children.emp:476` (`add.l d0, 4(sp)`) and `tile_cache.emp:611` (`move.w d0, 2(sp)`) are the ONLY two displaced-sp stores in the 122-file corpus (`children.emp:481`'s `add.l d0, (sp)` is the only plain-`(sp)` store, in the same already-bailing proc), and between them they account for all 3 of the 386 charged return sites the analysis does not track exactly (the other 383 are proven balanced). The displaced-sp READ is already exempt (a load cannot alter a slot). A narrow store exemption — a `d(sp)` write whose displacement lands within a tracked slot and whose width matches that slot, marking the slot opaque rather than bailing — would take coverage to 386/386 and would also un-bail whatever `preserves` claims those two procs lose. Measured, not guessed; not taken because 99.2% coverage did not justify widening a bailout in the same parcel that introduced an ERROR tier. — OPEN
- [bprime-2 lens C, 2026-08-04] **`bsr` was misread as a conditional branch by the SHARED CFG, and it was one local `bsr` away from breaking the build.** `flag_check::edges`' `is_cond_branch` test is `mnem.starts_with('b') && mnem.len() == 3`, which matches `bsr`. A `bsr .helper` therefore contributed the branch's TAKEN edge as well as its fall-through, splicing the helper's body into the caller's flow at the caller's state — so the helper's own `rts` was analyzed carrying the caller's stack. Closed in-parcel (`bsr` excluded; it calls and comes back, so its only successor is the fall-through). **Corpus-neutral by measurement** — all 9 corpus `bsr` sites target external symbols, and `jbsr`, the house idiom, is four letters and always fell to the plain-instruction arm. It is a PRE-EXISTING substrate bug that every `Cfg` consumer inherited (`flag_check`, `out_verify`, `context`, `preserves`); B′-2's ERROR-tier consumer is simply the first one for which it would have been fatal. Worth recording as a class: a three-letter mnemonic test is a spelling heuristic over an ISA that spells a call like a branch. — CLOSED
- [bprime-2 lenses B+C, 2026-08-04] **`Cfg::edges` uses ONE `Edge::Abandon` for a real return AND for control running off the end of a body**, so no consumer can tell them apart from the edge alone. B′-2 needed the distinction (a declared `falls_into` end is a continuation, not a return) and got it by re-reading the mnemonic at the exit site through a new `flag_check::is_return_mnemonic`. That works but it is a consumer re-deriving something the edge model knows and threw away. The structural fix is to split the variant (`Edge::Return` / `Edge::FallOff`); it was declined in-parcel because it touches every `edges` consumer and B′-2 is byte-frozen. Both lenses found the `falls_into` false positive INDEPENDENTLY, which is the tell that the ambiguity is the real defect and the symptom will recur. — **CLOSED** by the edge-split parcel (2026-08-05): `Edge::Return` / `Edge::FallOff`, decided in the builders. `preserves.rs`'s mnemonic re-read is deleted; `charge_fall_off_end` survives alone, as the per-proc DECLARATION it always was.

### B′-3a cycle budgets (2026-08-04) — what the path walk refuses, and what would change that

- [bprime-3a, 2026-08-04] **THE 68000 TIMING TABLE IS UNBUILT, and it is the follow-up parcel (B′-3b).** `@budget(cycles: N)` on a 68k proc reports `[cycles.unmodeled-cpu]` by name rather than measuring it against the Z80 table. The path walk is already CPU-parametric (it consumes a per-instruction cost and the shared `Cfg`'s CPU-specific edge model), so B′-3b is a cost function plus its cross-check, behind a fixed interface. **Source:** `oracle/Devices/M68000/*.h` in this workspace — the Exodus-derived core transcribes M68000UM Section 8's execution-time tables per opcode, mode by mode (`MOVE.h:13-24` is the full source×dest matrix, `ExecuteTime(cycles, busRead, busWrite)`), so every entry is cross-checkable against a shipping emulator rather than against memory. **THE DEMAND SITE IS NAMED AND IT IS 68k.** `dma_queue.emp:250-254`, `Process_DMA_Critical`: *"72 cycles/entry (3x move.l 20 + move.w 12), 576 for all 8 drain groups; **~670 whole-proc worst case** with dispatch, slot-var reset, and rts (68000 table timing — VDP FIFO waits can only add to this, never subtract)"* — a hand-derived whole-proc worst-case ceiling, in a proc that already carries two `ensure(...)` structure guards written to protect that very derivation (`:242`, `:248`). `@budget(cycles: 670)` is the declaration that comment is asking to become. Second, weaker: `sound_sfx.emp:606` `SfxDispatch` — *"it must be fast (the mailbox handler is on the timing-critical Z80 interrupt path)"* — a whole-proc obligation with the number missing, Z80 but loop-bearing. **Sizing (measured):** 235 of 419 corpus procs are structurally boundable (no call, no back edge) and the demand histogram is ~45 instruction families. **Two hazards B′-3b must answer and the Z80 half does not have:** (a) `mulu`/`muls`/`divu`/`divs` and register-count shifts are DATA-dependent, so they need a worst case or a refusal, never a nominal number; (b) a bare-`Sym` operand RELAXES at link time — `jbra`/`jbsr` emit four ordered candidate rungs and `RelaxAbsSym` a short/long pair — so a lowering-time cost must charge the longest rung or refuse, and that is a soundness argument, not data entry. — CLOSED (bprime-3b, 2026-08-05: the table shipped — `sigil-isa/m68k_cycles.rs` + the `sigil-frontend-emp/m68k_cycles.rs` classifier; the ruling is CHARGE THE DEAREST RUNG AND MARK IT A CEILING (`@budget` holds, `@cycles_exact` refuses via `[cycles.inexact-cost]`); both hazards answered (data-dependent forms carry documented maxima; DIVS adjudicated UM 158 over Exodus's 168 with oracle-next concurring). The named customer is honestly REFUSED — `Process_DMA_Critical` dispatches via `jmp .jump_table(a1)`, `[cycles.computed-transfer]` — and its ~670 prose ceiling is corroborated to the cycle by the same table; see the enumerated-dispatch row below.)
- [bprime-3a, 2026-08-04] **A loop cannot carry a budget, and the DAC streaming loop is the demand site.** The walk refuses any back edge (`[cycles.unbounded-loop]`) because the longest path through a cycle is unbounded. `z80_sound_driver.emp`'s `.loop` is exactly the shape a budget wants — its obligation is "one streaming PASS must be 195 T-states, the sample clock" — and it is expressed today by three hand-composed `ensure(cycles(prefix) + cycles(body) == N)` sums that name their three paths BY HAND (`:489-493`). The construct that would retire that hand composition is a PER-PASS budget (a back edge ends the path instead of unbounding it), or a declared trip count for the `djnz`/counted forms. Deliberately not built: a per-pass bound and a whole-proc bound are different claims, and letting one attribute mean either — silently, depending on the body — is exactly the wrong-contract hazard the refusals exist to prevent. Whoever builds it must give it its own spelling. AMENDED (bprime-3b, 2026-08-05): the demand is now TWO-CPU — `boot.emp:153`'s "~264 cycles" YM-delay `dbf` loop and `boot.emp:122`'s "~360k cycles" RAM clear are the corpus's only other stated 68k cycle claims, both loop-shaped, both refused today; and the 68k refusal distribution (90 of 211 refusals are back edges) says the per-pass form pays on the 68k side first. — OPEN
- [bprime-3a, 2026-08-04] **A call is refused rather than costed through the call graph.** `[cycles.opaque-call]` fires on `call`/`rst`; the callee's cost is a whole-program fact and the walk is per-file. `closure.rs` already carries per-proc facts up the call graph for registers and `requires`, and a `cycles` facet would compose the same way (max over the callee's paths, ⊤ at a blind/computed call). That is the P5 "one contract record, one propagation engine" shape the unification spec describes, and it is what would make a budget usable on anything above a leaf. Costs a corpus walk, so it belongs with the report parcel, not here. — OPEN
- [bprime-3a, 2026-08-04] **The Z80 T-state table is a DEMAND subset and 11 mnemonic families now block a boundable proc.** Measured over the sonic4 shape: `add` 42 sites, `ld` (forms outside the table) 35, `pop` 31, `srl` 21, `scf` 21, `set` 14, `rrca` 14, `res` 11, `ei` 7, `dec` (forms outside) 7, `bit` 7 — each the FIRST bail in some proc that is otherwise structurally boundable. Not added: the table's stated stance is that a form enters it when a timed region needs it, never speculatively, and no corpus proc is currently asking for a budget (see the packet's §4). Growing the table for a proc that is not asking would be manufacturing demand. The one Z80 proc that IS asking (`SfxDispatch`, above) is loop-bearing and so unboundable for a different reason, which is why table growth alone would not reach it. The list is here so a future adopter's cost is one measurement, not a rediscovery. — RECORDED
- [bprime-3a, 2026-08-04] **`ret` was missing from the Z80 T-state table** — a shipped model with one consumer had never been asked to cost a return, because `cycles(L1, L2)` measures INTERIOR spans and the driver's spans all end at a cut label. Any budgeted proc ends with one, so the second consumer found it immediately. Closed in-parcel (`ret` 10, `reti`/`retn` 14). Recorded because it is the third instance of the campaign's "a substrate with one consumer is only tested on the paths that consumer takes" finding (B′-2 §11 raised the general form). — CLOSED
- [bprime-3a lens C, 2026-08-04] **An unknown `@`-attribute was completely silent, and a declared PROOF is the worst thing to lose that way.** `@cycles_exakt` parsed, attached and was consumed by nobody: the reader sees a proof, the build performs none. **CLOSED in-parcel** — `validate_attr_form` now carries the closed set (`as_compat`, `allow`, `scaffolding`, `budget`, `cycles_exact`) and reports `[attr.unknown]`, matching what the section and module `(key: value)` header lists already do (`lower/mod.rs:1665`/`:1704`). `@align` / `@shape_divergent` are NOT in this family — they are consumed at their own syntactic positions and never reach the attribute parser, verified by grep over the corpus. A named argument to an attribute that takes none (`@allow(id: "x")`) is `[attr.form]` for the same reason. — CLOSED
- [bprime-3a lens C, 2026-08-04] **`pad_to_cycles` was the second Z80 cost model, and it is the EMITTING one.** `eval_pad_to_cycles` hard-coded `nop` = 4 T and `jr` = 12 T while `z80_cycles.rs`'s header claimed to be the sole authority — so a correction to the table would have left the pad emitting the old count while the balance `ensure`s silently re-derived around it. Closed in-parcel: both pad units now read `instr_cost` through `pad_unit_cost`, byte-neutral ×7 (the numbers agreed). Recorded because it is the sharpest instance yet of the campaign's extend-don't-duplicate rule being asserted in a header before it was true in the code — and it was caught by a lens checking the claim structurally rather than reading it. — CLOSED
- [bprime-3a, 2026-08-04] **A budget bounds ISSUED cycles, not elapsed time.** Bus contention (the Z80 losing the bus to a 68000 DMA, VDP-port wait states, ROM-bank access) is a whole-machine fact and is not modeled — which is the same unit the sound driver's own hand-derived balance proof uses, so the two agree. Neither are INTERRUPTS: a Z80 proc interrupted at `$0038` spends the handler's time on top of its budget, and `di`/`ei` are not in the T-state table so the guard cannot even be written inside a budgeted proc. Both stated in the module header rather than only here, but recorded because a budget's NAME invites the stronger reading and no diagnostic can correct it. — RECORDED

- [bprime-3a lenses A+B+C, 2026-08-04] **`Edge::Abandon` conflating a RETURN with a fall-off-end cost this parcel a soundness blocker, exactly as B′-2's ledger predicted it would recur.** `Cfg::z80_edges` returns `[Abandon, Abandon]` for a `ret cc` that ends a body — edge 0 is the taken return, edge 1 is control running off the end — and the budget walk's return test was keyed on the MNEMONIC, so both were closed as path ends and the escaping path was charged 5 T and declared finished. A too-LOW bound, on an unsuppressible ERROR-tier contract. Found independently by Lens B and Lens C with runnable repros; fixed by making the test per-EDGE and pinned both ways. **B′-2's row said "expect it to recur" and it recurred in the very next parcel**, in the opposite polarity (there a false positive, here a false negative). The structural fix — splitting `Edge::Return` / `Edge::FallOff` — is now asked for by two parcels; `is_return_mnemonic` gained a `cpu` parameter here, which is the half of it that could be done byte-frozen. — **CLOSED** by the edge-split parcel (2026-08-05). An end-of-body `ret cc` now presents `[Return, FallOff]`, so no rule keyed on the shared mnemonic can close both; `cycle_budget`'s positional `this_edge_returns` is deleted rather than migrated. Probe `a_tail_conditional_return_names_its_two_ends_apart` asserts the two edges DIFFER — demonstrably unsatisfiable against the single variant (own-run: `left: Abandon / right: Abandon`).
- [bprime-3a lens C, 2026-08-04] **`CodeItem::Inline` is invisible to every `Cfg` consumer.** A `DataBuf` spliced into a code stream is not an `Instr`, so `Cfg::build`'s `next_instr` links straight across it — while on hardware those bytes DECODE if control reaches them ($FF is `rst 38h`). The budget walk refuses on any inline data rather than costing it as free (`[cycles.inline-data]`), but the same blind spot is inherited unexamined by `preserves`, `flag_check`, `context` and `branch_const`, none of which has been asked what a data blob in a fall-through path means for its dataflow. Nobody has looked; the general answer is probably a reachability proof (data after an unconditional transfer is fine, data in a fall-through path is a defect), which no consumer has today. — OPEN
- [bprime-4, 2026-08-05] **SIX real `[call.slot-type-mismatch]` firings ship un-gated, because every corpus gate walks a define-free shape.** `--report contracts` under the sonic4 profile names them: `PState_Ground` ×2, `PState_Spindash` ×2, `Player_Animate`, `Player_Jump`, all `calls Sound_PlaySFX slot d0 expects SfxId but found an untyped value`. Every site is a `move.b #SFXID_*, d0` inside an `if SOUND_DRIVER_ENABLED == 1 { }` block, and `slot_type_corpus.rs` calls `analyze_corpus` with NO defines, so the block comptime-vanishes and `retrofitted_corpus_has_zero_slot_mismatches` passes on code it never saw. The engine's own sites (`sound_api.emp:384/387`, `animate.emp:228`, `game_debug.emp:110`) all bless correctly, so this is game-side drift, not a design hole. FIX = 6 `as SfxId` blessings (byte-neutral, type-layer) in aeon + route the gate through the target profile; they must land TOGETHER or the gate goes red. Kill-list row 103. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4, 2026-08-05] **The `out(rN if cc)` subcontract relation compares condition codes by canonical EQUALITY, not by implication.** `eq` implies `le` on 68k, so a target declaring `out(a1 if le)` genuinely does satisfy a bound promising `out(a1 if eq)`, and `subcontract_violations` now rejects that pair. The rejection is the safe polarity (the author spells the bound's own code) and an implication lattice is a per-CPU flag-semantics table — 16 codes on 68k, its own proof and its own tests — with zero corpus demand: the corpus declares three conditional outs, all `if eq`, and no bound demands one at all. DEMAND GATE: the first time a real target's code strictly implies a real bound's and the author must weaken the bound to compile. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4, 2026-08-05] **`out(a1, a1 if eq)` — a register mentioned BOTH unconditionally and under a guard — is modelled as conditional-only by `Contract`, contradicting §7.2's reading of it.** `contract_of_sig` builds `out` from `unconditional_outs` (which subtracts EVERY guarded register) and `out_cond` from the guard map (which keeps it), so the register lands only in the conditional half; §7.2 ruled the opposite ("their out is unconditional, whatever a second guarded mention says"), which is what `cond_out_pairs` implements for the overlap exemption. Two views of one declaration disagree. Not fixed here because the correction moves the register between two live relation terms — it would make the bound side STRICTER (demanding unconditional production) and the target side LOOSER (satisfying an unconditional demand), and neither direction has a corpus instance to prove against: zero corpus procs use the mixed form. TWO AMENDMENTS (lens C): the polarity is ASYMMETRIC and only one direction is safe — as a BOUND the demotion makes the relation UNDER-demand (a merely conditional target satisfies what the declaration promises unconditionally, a soundness hole), while as a TARGET it merely over-rejects (pessimism); and a RANGE hits it identically — `out(a0-a2, a1 if eq)` demotes a1 the same way, which is the counting case `cond_only_out_regs_of` exists for, so the row is not about the literal `out(a1, a1 if eq)` spelling. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4, 2026-08-05] **FIVE transitive clobber under-declarations ship in the DEBUG shape, un-gated for the same reason.** `--report contracts --game sonic4 --debug` fires `Collected_ParkSlot direct d2`, `EntityWindow_TrySpawnRing direct d5`, and `EntityWindow_{RescanRings,ScanRingsRight,PopulateSectionRings} transitive d5`; the plain shape fires zero. `corpus_closure_residue_is_empty_the_error_gate` runs `analyze_corpus_with` under `GAME_CONFIG_DEFINES` only — no `DEBUG` — so the ERROR gate that exists to stop exactly this has never seen the debug arms. D1c agrees the values are LIVE (`Collected_UpdateCenter holds d2 across clobber`, `EntityWindow_{RescanY,Scan} holds d5`). Both sites do save and restore by hand (`entity_window.emp:368/380` a `.w` pair around a `moveq`-clobbered d2; `:859/861` a `movem.l` pair around d5) and neither declares the `preserves`, so the closure charges the write correctly and the contracts are the thing that is wrong. RUNTIME IMPACT is nil today — the ParkSlot caller reads d2 only through `.w` ops, so the half-width restore covers it — but the contract says otherwise and nothing checks. FIX = declare the verified `preserves` (widen ParkSlot's save to `.l` first) + run the closure gate under the shipping defines; same land-together constraint as kill-list row 103. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4 lens C, 2026-08-05] **`subcontract_violations` licenses only `target.clobbers`; `target.out` and `target.out_cond` are unlicensed write channels.** The relation has five terms and none checks the target's RESULT registers against the bound's write license. Measured own-run: bound `clobbers(d0) out(a1 if eq)` (a1 ∉ clobbers, so §7.1 says a1 is PRESERVED on the ¬eq edge) vs target `clobbers(d0) out(a1)` returns NO violation — yet the target writes a1 on every path, destroying it exactly where the bound promised it survives. Same hole for a target carrying an EXTRA guard (`out(a1 if eq, a1 if ne)`) against the same bound, and for a target adding an unrelated `out(d2)` the bound never mentions. `check_firings` treats `out` as a write set from the caller's view, so these ARE writes. NOT a regression — master accepts the identical pairs, and the missing term predates the cc comparison; corpus-unreachable (no bound declares a conditional out, 0 `[contract.hook-signature]` in any green build). The term wants to be `target.out ∪ target.out_cond.keys() ⊆ bound.clobbers ∪ bound.out`, refined so a target guard on `cc` is licensed by a bound guard on the same `cc`. It is deferred rather than added because `unconditional_out_satisfies_a_conditional_bound_out` — the strengthening escape §7.2's whole error tier leans on — asserts one of the accepted pairs, so adding the term is a ruling on the escape, not a bug fix. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4 lens C, 2026-08-05] **`[proc.out-cond-invalid]` never runs on an `extern proc` or a contract-type signature, and the cc just became load-bearing there.** `check_out_flags_cond` has exactly one caller (`lower/proc.rs:186`, over a `ProcDecl`), and `VALID_CCS` is referenced nowhere else; the pinning test uses `proc P`, so the gap is untested. Before the guards map a typo'd cc in a signature was inert — the relation ignored codes. Now it decides conformance: `type Alloc = proc () out(a1 if eqq)` makes every target violate with `produces conditional output 'a1' on 'eq', not on 'eqq'` and no root-cause diagnostic, and a typo shared by bound and target "verifies" a guard that means nothing. Fail-safe polarity (loud, not silent) and latent (0 externs in the corpus, no signature declares a conditional out). FIX = run the cc validity check over `ProcSig` wherever a signature is bound. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4 lens A/B, 2026-08-05] **Retiring `emp_contracts` cost the census its ARBITRARY-FILE-LIST mode, and nothing replaced it.** `emp_contracts a.emp b.emp -D X=1` ran the whole-corpus contract analysis over any file set with any defines. `--report contracts` requires a scannable aeon manifest and one of five fixed target profiles, so pointing the closure census at a scratch file, a reduced repro, or a non-aeon tree is no longer possible with any shipped tool. This compounds the recorded `[bprime-0b lens C]` observation that a Sigil consumer outside the aeon tree is not backstopped. Fixed profiles are the right DEFAULT; the ask is that they not be the only option (`--report contracts --files a.emp b.emp -D X=1`). Same row carries `emp_census`'s KILL CONDITION: it is the surviving `src/bin/` sibling and emits TSV over an arbitrary file list — exactly the capability dropped — so it follows `--report` only once `--report` grows either a file-list input or a machine-readable output mode, not before. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4 lens A, 2026-08-05] **The report is text with no locations, hand-set column widths, and no machine-readable form.** (a) No section carries `path:line:col`; the three that carry spans print raw global byte offsets. The consequence is visible: two identical `PState_Ground calls Sound_PlaySFX` rows a user cannot tell apart or navigate to, and `BG_Init with z80_stopped` ×2. `run_contract_report` already builds a `SourceIndex` and calls `render_program_diags` — the machinery is in scope and unused. (b) `{:<28}` / `{:<24}` are repeated ~15 times as magic literals and demonstrably overflow (`GameState_ObjectTestChurn_Init`, `TileCache_FindStagedBlock`), with one row padded by literal trailing spaces to fake alignment. (c) The new gate asserts on the literal string `-- dropped instructions (must be 0): 0 --` because there is nothing structural to assert on, so any render cleanup breaks it. All three dissolve together if `ContractReport` yields span-carrying rows the CLI renders through the existing diagnostic path, with a `--report-format text|json` for gates. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4 lens A/B, 2026-08-05] **Four accessors over one concept, varying on axes no name encodes.** `cond_out_regs` (any-guard, regs), `cond_only_out_regs` (exclusively-conditional, regs), `cond_out_pairs` (exclusively-conditional, reg+cc, declaration order), `cond_out_guards` (any-guard, reg→{cc}). The names give a reader no way to derive which keying or which payload they select, and the doc comments carry the whole distinction. The grid wants to be `<keying>_<payload>`: `cond_out_{regs,guards}` and `cond_only_out_{regs,guards}`, with `cond_out_pairs` folded into the last (its only extra is declaration order, which neither consumer depends on — both immediately `filter_map(Reg::from_name)`). Deferred because renaming reaches `cond_out_pairs`' consumers in `lower/proc.rs` and `corpus_contracts.rs`. `ProcDecl::cond_out_regs` is ALREADY dead (zero src callers, pre-dates this parcel) and dies with the rename. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4 lens B, 2026-08-05] **`canonical_cc` is a string-level patch over a missing type, and there are now five other 68000 cc-normalization sites.** `value::Cc` already exists, already has no `Hs`/`Lo` variants (it normalizes by construction), and already has two independent `&str -> Cc` parsers (`lower/code.rs:1605`, `sigil-frontend-as/src/eval.rs:4793`). Meanwhile the alias fold is spelled inline at `flag_check::negate_cc`, `out_verify::Flags::{refine,eval}`, and `branch_const::cc_holds`. Carrying `ast::CondResult::cc` as a `Cc` instead of a `String` would retire `canonical_cc`, delete the alias arms at all four, and turn `VALID_CCS` from a membership test into a parse. (`flag_check::{invalid_edge,valid_edge}` were routed through `canonical_cc` in this parcel because one of them was a live false negative; the other four are byte-neutral tidying with no bug behind them.) Also spotted while checking: `branch_const.rs:151` has a copy-pasted `// C == 0` comment on the `"cs" | "lo"` (C=1) arm. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4 lens B, 2026-08-05] **The target-to-profile mapping is spelled six times, and the five label strings with it.** `BuildTarget::label_and_profile` (sigil-cli) now serves the build and both reports, but `warn_tier_corpus.rs:110`, `derive_offcanon.rs:92`, `native_offcanonical_rom.rs:108`, `native_offcanonical_full.rs:186`, and `native_offcanonical_placement.rs:108` each carry their own 5-to-7 row table, and `"sonic4 plain"` / `"demo debug"` / `"config_a"` / `"config_b"` / `"lean"` are minted in all six. There is no `label -> profile` selector in `sigil-harness/src/native.rs`, which is the natural home: `native::profile_for_shape(&str)` (or a `Shape` enum) with all six reading it. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4 lens B, 2026-08-05] **`--report contracts` scopes its DEFINES to the target but not its MODULE SET.** `run_ram_report` scopes (`region_ids = ["engine.ram", profile.game_ram_module]`); the contract report takes every module the manifest scan finds, so `--game demo` analyzes `PState_Ground` / `Player_Jump` / all of `games/sonic4/` under demo's `MAX_RING_BUFFER=16 / SOUND_DRIVER_ENABLED=0` — a shape no ROM ships. `GameProfile::registry` carries the per-target module ids, so the input exists. Deferred deliberately: every pre-existing corpus gate walks `engine/` + `games/` whole, so tightening the report first would make it disagree with all of them; it should land with the same change that routes those gates through the profile (kill-list row 103). — RECORDED
- [bprime-4-gates, 2026-08-05] **The `.emp` contract vocabulary cannot say "preserves the low word".** Both DEBUG-shape under-declarations this parcel fixed are the same shape: `Collected_ParkSlot` brackets its duplicate-id scan with `move.w d2,-(sp)` / `move.w (sp)+,d2` and `EntityWindow_TrySpawnRing` brackets its no-dup scan with `move.w d5,-(sp)` / `move.w (sp)+,d5`, while the bracketed code (`moveq #N, dK`) writes all 32 bits. The `preserves` verifier is width-correct and rejects the half-width round-trip, so the only sayable contracts are `clobbers(dK)` (true, and what shipped here — byte-neutral) or a `.l`-widened save plus `preserves(dK)` (also true, byte-CHANGING in the three DEBUG shapes: `$3F02`→`$2F02` and `$3F05`→`$2F05`, same length, so `refreeze` chain 44 would still have to bump). Neither expresses what the code actually guarantees and what all three callers actually consume — `Collected_UpdateCenter` reads d2 only through `sub.w`/`cmpi.w`, and `EntityWindow_{Scan,RescanY}` read d5 only through `btst d6, d5` with d6 < 4 — which is "the low word survives". A `preserves.w(dK)` (or `preserves(dK: Word)`) would let the declaration match the code instead of forcing a choice between over-charging and re-freezing. DEMAND GATE: a second corpus instance, or the first caller that genuinely needs the long. — RECORDED — **RESOLVED (partial-width preserves parcel, 2026-08-06): the vocabulary shipped as `preserves(dN.w)`** (a dotted data-register facet through the existing reg-list grammar — the `sr.mask`/`sr.ccr` precedent; `.b`/`aN.w`/`.l`/unknown each refuse with their own arm). Both DEBUG-arm sites this row names now declare it (`Collected_ParkSlot` d2.w, `EntityWindow_TrySpawnRing` d5.w). See row 2144's CLOSED annotation and the spec.
- [bprime-4-gates, 2026-08-05] **A `clobbers` clause cannot be comptime-conditional, so a DEBUG-only register effect is charged in every shape.** The five declarations this parcel widened (`Collected_ParkSlot` d2; `EntityWindow_{TrySpawnRing,RescanRings,ScanRingsRight,PopulateSectionRings}` d5) describe writes that exist ONLY under `DEBUG == 1`; the plain shapes now carry a clobber charge for a register nothing writes. It is sound (over-declaring a write never loses safety) and measurably free today — the ×7 byte bar is unchanged and no new firing appeared in any shape — but it is a real precision loss in the four shapes that actually ship to players, and it will grow with every DEBUG-gated invariant scan the engine adds. `proc P () clobbers(d0-d1, a1) clobbers(d2) if DEBUG == 1` (or a `when` on the clause) is the shape; the closure would then need a per-shape effective map, which it already effectively has since the walk is per-shape. Deferred: no measured cost yet, and the language round should rule on clause-level comptime before one clause gets it. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4-gates, 2026-08-05] **`movem_restore_guard_corpus` / `preserves_corpus` / `dead_save_corpus` are define-free and cannot be fixed where they live.** All three pass `&[]` as the define set (`eval_proc_body(..., &[], ...)` / `analyze_corpus`) and all three sit in `crates/sigil-frontend-emp/tests/`. `sigil-harness` — the only owner of `GameProfile` and therefore of any real `-D` set — DEPENDS on `sigil-frontend-emp`, so those tests structurally cannot reach a profile; the fix is to move them to `crates/sigil-cli/tests/` (where `warn_tier_corpus` and the two gates flipped this parcel live) or to give the frontend a define-set injection point that does not import the harness. `movem_restore_guard_corpus` is the one with teeth today: it pairs `movem` saves against restores over the whole corpus and every `movem` pair inside an `if DEBUG == 1 { }` arm is invisible to it. — **CLOSED 2026-08-06 (lane hyg): RELOCATED-AND-WIDENED, the overseer's relocate-not-invert ruling.** All three moved to `crates/sigil-cli/tests/` (where `warn_tier_corpus` and the two bprime-4 gates already live) and re-run per shipped shape under `native::shape_defines` + the shape's bound `InterfaceEnv` (`bind_corpus_interfaces`), so DEBUG/CRASH_REPORT/SOUND-gated arms are scanned exactly when the ROM that ships them turns the arm on. NON-VACUITY (before → after, per gate): `movem_restore_guard` GREW — define-free baseline 32 restores → widest shape 33 (the +1 is a `SOUND_DRIVER_ENABLED`-gated `movem (sp)+` restore that no-define lowering dropped; sonic4-plain/debug/config_a/lean = 33, the sound-off config_b and demo shapes = 32), the exemption-tripwire property re-proven under every shape; `dead_save` = 3 → 3 identical across all seven shapes with the DEBUG arms counted (the three known customers TestChurnObj_Main/A0 + TileCache_FillColumn/D7 + TileCache_WarmupBelowRow/D7 — no gated dead-save was hiding); `preserves` = 6 define-free evals → 42 (6 residue procs × 7 shapes), every one matching its prediction, proving the checkpoint shape-STABLE across the 3 DEBUG shapes rather than an artifact of the empty define set. No structural wall hit — sigil-cli already depends on both sigil-harness and sigil-frontend-emp. Byte-neutral (tests only).
- [bprime-4-gates lens B, 2026-08-05] **Zero dropped instructions does NOT prove a profile's defines reached the analysis, and it is the only thing that was checking.** `the_contracts_report_is_wired_and_carries_the_targets_defines` asserted exactly that. Measured false: a statement-`if` whose condition evaluates to `Value::Poison` (`eval/asm.rs:341-350`) returns having discarded BOTH arms, contributing nothing to `dropped_instrs`. Repro — `proc Foo () clobbers(d0) { moveq #0,d0; if NOPE_UNDEFINED == 0 { moveq #1,d2 }; rts }` reports 0 drops and 0 firings (the d2 write is invisible), while the same proc with the write unguarded reports `Foo direct d2`. So a profile that lost or misspelled `SOUND_DRIVER_ENABLED` or `DEBUG` outright reproduces the exact blind spot the shape walk exists to close, at zero drops. What the drop count DOES catch is a missing VALUE define (`MAX_RING_BUFFER` absent → `DrawRings` cannot lower `vram_art(...)` → 1 drop). The only toggle tripwires are the two shape-sensitivity probes, which cover `SOUND_DRIVER_ENABLED` and `DEBUG` and nothing else — `CRASH_REPORT`, `SOUND_DEBUG_HOTKEYS`, `SOUND_DBG_MIRROR` have no proof they reach the analysis at all. FIX = a `[comptime.unresolved]` surface: the set of names a comptime condition referenced that the define set failed to resolve, on `ContractReport`, pinned empty per shape. That makes "add a new toggle" fail loudly instead of silently re-opening the hole, which a per-toggle probe never will. The doc claim is corrected in-place; the surface is not built here. — RECORDED — **RESOLVED (comptime-unresolved parcel, 2026-08-05): the surface SHIPPED, pinned EMPTY per shape.** `ContractReport::comptime_unresolved` collects `(proc, name, span)` AT the two `if`-condition eval sites (statement `AsmStmt::If` + expression `eval_if`), fed by the `unknown name`→`Poison` resolution-miss sites in `eval_path` — no second walk, so it cannot drift from what the analysis evaluated. `corpus_comptime_conditions_all_resolve_the_error_gate` pins it empty across all seven shapes; the row's own repro is executable twice (`a_poisoned_statement_if_is_invisible_at_zero_drops_and_the_surface_names_it` synthetic + `a_misspelled_toggle_in_a_condition_is_named_at_zero_drops_in_every_shape` corpus-scale, both asserting ZERO drops while the surface fires). The three unproven toggles now have per-shape teeth: CRASH_REPORT surfaces in exactly the 4 plain shapes (|| short-circuit), SOUND_DBG_MIRROR in exactly sonic4-debug+config_a (&&), SOUND_DEBUG_HOTKEYS fails the interface BIND in the 5 sonic4-family shapes (its one site is sonic4's implement group). `--report contracts` renders the section. See `2026-08-05-comptime-unresolved.md`.
- [bprime-4-gates lens B, 2026-08-05] **`if SFXID_REV_LOOP >= 0` (`engine/sound/sound_sfx.emp:796`) is the first comptime arm NO shape walk can reach.** `SFXID_REV_LOOP` is a corpus const (`games/sonic4/config/sound_ids.emp:74` = `SFXID_SPINDASH` = `$AB`), not a `-D`, so it holds the same value in all seven shipped shapes and the documented `-1 = feature off` arm is unreachable by any per-shape walk — the shape axis simply does not have an axis for it. Harmless today (it is a Z80 module, skipped by the 68k closure pass) and named because it is the SHAPE the next one will take: the seven profiles cover both polarities of all five `-D` toggles, so the residual blind arms are the ones gated on corpus CONSTANTS rather than on defines. A game whose config sets `SFXID_REV_LOOP = -1` would be the first real instance, and the answer is either a config-matrix axis or a per-arm compile probe, not more shapes. — RECORDED
- [bprime-4-gates lens C, 2026-08-05] **The corpus gates' aeon anchors are exact source strings including interior whitespace.** The four negative probes assert on literals like `"moveq   #SFXID_JUMP, d0 as SfxId"` and `"proc Collected_ParkSlot () clobbers(d0-d2, a1) preserves(a0) {"`. A whitespace-only reformat in aeon, or a re-spelling the language accepts as equivalent (`clobbers(d0-d2/a1)` for `clobbers(d0-d2, a1)`), panics the sigil gate from the other repo. The failure is LOUD and names the anchor — deliberately, and it is the right polarity — but it is cross-repo coupling on formatting, and it will bite the first aeon-side formatter. The durable form is doctoring the parsed AST (drop a register from a named proc's clobber set, strip a bless from a named site) rather than the source text, which needs a small mutation helper over `ast::File` that no test currently has. — RECORDED
- [overseer ruling, 2026-08-05] **DEMAND 1 (partial-width register preservation) now has instances in THREE tranches, and the conservative-`clobbers` resolution is re-affirmed as standing.** The `define-gates` lane's five DEBUG arms save and restore only the LOW WORD of d5 (`move.w d5,-(sp)` around a `moveq`), which `preserves.rs:470` correctly refuses to credit — it sets the entry-value bit only on a `.l` full-register round-trip. The lane's lens C dissented and argued for widening the saves to `.l` plus `preserves(d5)`, correctly noting the cost is only three golden recaptures because the size tables do not move. **RULED: the porter's byte-neutral `clobbers` stands, per the already-ratified t34 resolution (row above, `Player_Main`'s `move.w d7,-(sp)`, reconfirmed by the t39 panel as "the IDENTICAL already-ratified pattern").** The principle the two rulings share: DO NOT CHANGE MACHINE CODE TO WORK AROUND A KNOWN GAP IN A VERIFIER'S MODEL. Widening five saves to `.l` spends ROM bytes and cycles preserving high words no reader consumes, purely so a checker that cannot express partial-width preservation stops objecting; both spellings are true statements, and only one of them leaves the program alone. The cost argument is sound and is not the deciding axis — a byte-changing edit is cheap here, but it is still an edit made FOR the tool rather than for the program. **THE ASK IS UNCHANGED AND NOW BETTER-EVIDENCED:** a partial-width preserve model (sub-register save/restore tracking, or a `preserves(d5.w)` spelling). Demand sites: t34 `Player_Main` (d7), t39 `TestPlayer` (d7, same pattern), and these five DEBUG arms (d5). Until it is modelled, the conservative full-register `clobbers` plus a documenting site comment is the house answer, and a lane proposing otherwise should cite this row. — OPEN (demand 1; three tranches; conservative `clobbers` stands until modelled) — **CLOSED (partial-width preserves parcel, 2026-08-06; spec `2026-08-06-partial-width-preserves-design.md`).** The model gained the width: `preserves(dN.w)` is a data-register FACET claim proven by a `Width::W` (or `.l`) save/restore round-trip at the credit site (`preserves.rs:792`, the drifted `:470` corrected — the single-bool `entry` bit became `entry`+`entry_word`, each slot carrying its `save_width` so a `.b` round-trip credits neither). Conservative v1: a `.w`-preserving callee is a FULL clobber to every consumer — `dN` joins `declared_clobbers`/the local allowed set (verdicts identical to `clobbers(dN)`), is NOT credited to `verified_preserves`, and the low-word round-trip is a separate obligation (per-file byte gate DEFERS a call-blocked claim; the corpus `word_preserve_firings` oracle gate is its final authority). CENSUS-CORRECTION CATCH: the spec's "five DEBUG arms / seven witnesses" was wrong — only FOUR procs round-trip the word in their OWN bodies (`Player_Main` d7, `TestPlayer_Main` d7 [NOT `TestPlayer`, whose init body never touches d7 and falls into `TestPlayer_Main`], `Collected_ParkSlot` d2, `EntityWindow_TrySpawnRing` d5); the other three EntityWindow procs (`ScanRingsRight`/`PopulateSectionRings`/`RescanRings`) carry d5 TRANSITIVELY through `jbsr TrySpawnRing`, so under conservative v1 they must NOT flip and stay `clobbers(d0-d5)` — the real-corpus witnesses for the conservative-refusal pin (spec §6 amendment ratified the split). Byte-neutral ×7; adoption flipped the four; all four verify under the oracle in both plain (never-written for the DEBUG-gated d2/d5) and debug (round-trip) shapes.

### Edge split (2026-08-05) — what one builder still does not say, and who is still asking the mnemonic

- [edge-split, 2026-08-05] **THREE consumers collapse `Return` and `FallOff` into one arm with NO `falls_into` policy, and the split is the moment each of them gained the variant needed to state one.** `preserves::BalanceObserver` is the only consumer parameterized on the declaration (`charge_fall_off_end`, plumbed from `proc.falls_into.is_none()` at `lower/proc.rs:257`). The other three charge a fall-off-end as a return unconditionally: `out_verify`'s exit arm charges the `out(rN)` claim where control has NOT returned and the `falls_into` SUCCESSOR may be what produces rN (**false positive**, `[proc.out-unverified]`, ERROR tier); `preserves::ReturnScope::AllReturns` obligates the entry-value proof there; `z80_preserves` checkpoints the register file there. All three are pre-existing — the old single variant covered both cases identically — and all three are the safe polarity (over-obligation, never a silent pass), which is why nothing fires on the corpus. The structural answer is that `charge_fall_off_end` wants to be a field of the WALK, not of one observer: `run_stack_dataflow` and its Z80 sibling should take the proc's `falls_into` declaration and every consumer should read one answer. — OPEN
- [edge-split lens C, 2026-08-05] **`out_verify::is_uncond_tail` re-derives from the mnemonic what a `Defer` split would state — the same defect class this parcel closed for `Return`/`FallOff`, in the same enum.** `Cfg::edges` emits `Defer` from exactly two arms (an UNCONDITIONAL tail transfer, and a conditional branch whose target is external) and the consumer recovers which by re-reading the mnemonic through a private 68k table (`bra|jbra|jmp|jra`) that duplicates `flag_check::UNCOND_MNEMONICS`. Correct today — 68k-only consumer, lists identical — and it is the fourth site of the class with the two lists unsynchronized. Drift polarity: `is_uncond_tail` false where the builder meant a tail ⇒ the required-return path is skipped ⇒ **false negative** on `[proc.out-unverified]`. The fix is `Edge::TailOut` / `Edge::BranchOut`, which deletes the helper. DELIBERATELY DECLINED in-parcel and the argument is in the packet §4: `Return`/`FallOff` are two facts about the MACHINE, while `Defer`'s remaining flavours turn on whether the TARGET returns — which the builder cannot know and must not guess. Only the conditional/unconditional axis is builder-visible, and that is a property of the terminator. — **CLOSED (lane E, 2026-08-06).** `Edge::Defer` is DELETED and replaced by `Edge::TailOut` (the successor of an unconditional transfer out; always an instruction's only edge) and `Edge::BranchOut` (a conditional terminator's taken side out; never an instruction's only edge). The axis is passed into the shared `branch_edge` three-way from the call site as an `OutFlavor`, because the three-way sees only operands. `is_uncond_tail` is GONE — the fourth mnemonic table of the family deleted — and `out_verify`'s two arms now read the flavor off the edge. Neither variant claims anything about the TARGET: that bounding argument is inherited verbatim, so no consumer relitigates it. Corpus census MEASURED by instrumenting the arms and rebuilding: `out_verify` executes the `TailOut` credit arm 48 times (12 fixpoint iterations × 4 sites — `Art_Decompress`'s `jbra ZX0_Decompress` and `jbra S4LZ_Decompress`, plus two computed `jmp PcRelIdx` tails inside `S4LZ_Decompress`) and the `BranchOut` arm ZERO times, confirming the spec's §6 prediction exactly; `terminal_external_tail` fires on `jbra Sound_PostByte` ×12 and `jbra QueueDMA_Deferrable.transfer` ×4; `preserves`'s tail-clobber marking sees 39 `TailOut` / 5 `BranchOut` in the plain s4 shape and 108 / 14 in the s4.debug shape (shape-scoped — see the lane-E witness row below for the two `BranchOut` sites and why neither changes a verdict). Byte-neutral across all seven golden targets at chain 49; warn-tier id sets and counts unmoved on every shape. This deletion also shrinks the ISA-crate `is_call`/`is_return`/`is_branch` classifier row's evidence base by one instance; that row stays OPEN and absorbs nothing.
- [edge-split lens C, 2026-08-05] **`z80_bus::check_bus_state` holds a `Cfg` and consults its OWN private 68k return table instead of asking it.** `z80_bus.rs:120` copies `RETURN_MNEMONICS`; `:258` walks `items` linearly testing membership, while `:208` has already built the `Cfg` and run `must_in_states` over it. Correct today (68k check, 68k table) — but it is the one exit classifier in the crate outside the edge model, and it therefore never sees the other exits the model would hand it: an `Edge::Defer` (a `bra External` with the bus stopped) and an `Edge::FallOff` never reach the `[bus.stopped-at-return]` test. **False negative.** Fix: ask `cfg.edges(idx)` for `Edge::Return`, and decide explicitly what the other two exits owe. — OPEN
- [edge-split lens C, 2026-08-05] **`flag_check::{invalid_edge, valid_edge}` are wholesale 68k with no fence, and `check_flag_unused` is ALREADY wired for Z80.** Both walk a straight line testing the bare `RETURN_MNEMONICS`, `writes_carry(_, Cpu::M68000)`, `branch_cond` (68k `bXX`), `UNCOND_MNEMONICS` and `cc_transparent`. Not reachable from Z80 today: `check_result_invalid_path` and `conditional_out_edge_credits` are called only from the 68k `proc_bufs` loop, and `corpus_contracts.rs:413` records the boundary in prose. So this is NOT B′-3a's defect recurring — it becomes it silently the moment `[call.result-invalid-path]` is wired for Z80, which is the natural next parcel since the flag check already is. A Z80 `ret` between a call and its guard fails `RETURN_MNEMONICS.contains` and the walk steps PAST the return. Smallest fence: a `Cpu` parameter with a `debug_assert!`, or the explicit 68k-only banner `check_regions` already carries. — OPEN
- [edge-split, 2026-08-05] **The mnemonic-SHAPE heuristic is load-bearing in two builders and has now been excepted by name twice.** `Cfg::edges` classifies a conditional branch as `starts_with('b') && len() == 3 && mnem != "bsr"`, and `Cfg::z80_edges` had to drop `"call"` out of its `jp|jr|call` conditional arm for the identical reason. Both exceptions are correct and both are spelling tests standing in for an ISA fact. The structural answer is a per-CPU classifier in the ISA crate (`is_call` / `is_return` / `is_branch` in `sigil_ir::backend`) that the frontend consults instead of reading letters; `no_call_mnemonic_yields_a_transfer_out_edge` pins the property for the five call spellings in the meantime. — OPEN
- [edge-split, 2026-08-05] **`Cfg` treats `CodeItem::Inline` as invisible, and the edge split does not touch that.** Restating the B′-3a row because this parcel enumerated every builder arm and confirmed it: `next_instr` links straight across a `DataBuf` spliced into a code stream, so `FallOff` means "no next INSTRUCTION", not "no next byte". On hardware those bytes decode. Unchanged by the split and unexamined by every consumer except `cycle_budget`, which refuses. — OPEN (see the B′-3a row)
- [comptime-unresolved, 2026-08-05] **The surface's first real outing found a live blind spot the ledger row had not predicted: `Camera_Update`'s landing-lock arm — code that SHIPS in every sonic4-family ROM — was invisible to the whole contract analysis in every shape.** The arm is gated `if Game.CAMERA_JUMP_LOCK { }` on an L1 interface member, and every corpus walk ran with `InterfaceEnv::empty()` — the member never resolved, the condition poisoned, both arms discarded at zero drops, in all seven shapes, and nothing knew (this is ledger row ~2124's "gated on corpus constants, not defines" class, except the arm is REACHED: sonic4 binds `true`). FIXED same parcel, the class way: `analyze_corpus_with_contracts` threads a bound `InterfaceEnv`; `bind_corpus_interfaces` builds it per shape over engine + the SHAPE'S OWN game's modules (bind demands exactly one `implement`, the corpus holds one per game) with the PASS-1 corpus env as bind ambient (`bind_with_ambient` / `eval_expr_in_file_ambient` — impl values reference imported consts). Both shape-walking gate files + `--report contracts` now bind per shape; `an_interface_gated_arm_is_walked_under_each_shapes_own_game` plants an undeclared write inside the arm and demands fires-in-5-sonic4-shapes/silent-in-2-demo-shapes. Measured blast radius of the arm entering the analysis: ZERO — every firing family, D1c (21/26), dead-saves (3), and the context censuses (23/20) unchanged. HONEST RESIDUALS, none blocking: (1) a condition evaluated UNDER a label-value context (immediate/data-init/call-arg) resolves an unknown bareword as a deferred link Label, not a miss — outside the surface's reach; the build rejects it as a one-file type error, and every corpus statement-`if` evaluates outside label ctx (pinned by the expression-if repro test's doc). (2) implement-GROUP conditions are evaluated by the BIND, not the walk — a lost define there surfaces as a bind ERROR (`analyze_every_shape` asserts bind-error-free; the hotkeys probe pins it), not on `ContractReport`. (3) the define-free `corpus_report()` family stays env-free — row 103 owns that flip, and it has no shape to pick a game by. (4) module-level `ensure`/`equ`/data-initializer conditions are evaluated only when a walked proc body reaches them — item-level comptime conditions outside any proc have no surface row of their own. — LEDGERED (residuals); the headline is FIXED.
- [comptime-unresolved lens C, 2026-08-05] **The un-harvested `Poison` sites the surface does not yet cover — the "Poison is a silence budget" census.** The parcel harvests the two `if`-condition sites and (post-panel) the memoized const/equ read PLUS both loop heads (`eval_while`'s condition, `eval_for`'s iterable — lens B and C converged on the loops; harvested same parcel, `a_poisoned_loop_head_lands_on_the_surface` pins it); the arms still un-covered: (b) `eval_match` (pattern.rs) — a Poison scrutinee discards every arm; (c) the `with … if` bracket gate (asm.rs ~483) — a Poison gate lowers the body BARE (opposite polarity: code stays visible but the Enter/Exit context marks vanish, so the bracket census under-counts and the unresolved name never surfaces); (d) the typed intercepts in `eval_path` steps 1b–3c (hook-as-value, missing enum variant / offsets / dispatch / table member) error-without-noting, and the corpus walk discards diags — narrow, the build rejects each one-file; (e) `resolve_data_value`'s Poison memo (the `Def.field` value read) — the data-item sibling of the closed const memo door. Each is a three-line `note_unresolved_name` (or a widened surface name) plus a synthetic test; (c) is the interesting one because its failure mode is a silently smaller `context_regions` census, which IS a pinned number. Also: `seam1.rs`'s doctored Z80 harness still evals with `InterfaceEnv::empty()` — fine while no Z80 module reads an interface member; thread the env when next touched. And lens B's durable fact: **comptime fn BODIES inherit the caller's `label_ctx`** (`call_fn_with_values` does not reset it, unlike `resolve_const`), so the first define-gated `if` inside a fn called from an immediate/call-arg position resolves its miss as a link Label and enters the blind zone silently — today's operand-position fns (`vdp_comm`/`dma_length`/`plane_loc`) are if-free and the if-carrying emit tools are spliced at statement position, so exposure is zero; the candidate fix is resetting `label_ctx` across fn-body entry (or suspending it across condition eval), which is a semantics question for the next evaluator parcel, not a patch. — LEDGERED (kill: the next parcel touching the evaluator's Poison discipline takes the census as its worklist).
- [bprime-3b, 2026-08-05] **A computed jump-table dispatch cannot carry a budget, and the corpus's ONLY stated whole-proc 68k demand site is one.** `Process_DMA_Critical` and `Player_SetState` both dispatch via `jmp .table(a1)` and are refused `[cycles.computed-transfer]` — correctly, since the destination set is data. But in BOTH procs the table's geometry is already structurally pinned by author-written `ensure(...)` guards (slot stride, slot count), so the dispatch's target set IS finite and provable; a declared enumerated-dispatch form ("this jmp lands on one of these N labels") would turn both refusals into measurements, and `Process_DMA_Critical`'s prose "~670 whole-proc worst case" — corroborated to the cycle by the bprime-3b table arithmetic (ceiling 670, post-relaxation 662) — into a checked `@budget`. Needs its own spelling and a soundness story for the claim that the list is exhaustive. — CLOSED (enum-dispatch, 2026-08-05: the form is `targets(.a, .b, …)`, a trailing clause on an unconditional COMPUTED transfer — spec `specs/2026-08-05-enumerated-dispatch-design.md`. Soundness story (§2): exhaustiveness is the AUTHOR's claim; the compiler verifies existence/locality/distinctness of every named label and NOTHING reads the clause but the opt-in cycle-budget walk, so a wrong enumeration can mis-measure only the budget it feeds and corrupts no other analysis. `Process_DMA_Critical`'s jmp now carries `targets(.slot_0..8)` — nine zero-byte labels at the PHYSICAL landing points (`.jump_table + k·14`) — and `@budget(cycles: 670)`, the walk's measured dearest arm. The three numbers MEET: prose ~670 / B′-3b table 670 / walk 670. (The first cut named the DOWNSTREAM drain labels and measured 646, 24 under the true ceiling — an unsound budget the hardware exceeds; the landing-label fix charges the slot-8 dispatch leas the drain spelling skipped. Regression pin: `targets_charge_the_landed_on_code_not_a_downstream_label`.) The labels are ROM-neutral (every golden anchor held); they grow the deb2 symbol table, an appendix-only full_crc drift provenance.toml blesses. The EXHAUSTIVE-by-construction successor — where a soundness-bearing analysis may see through the dispatch — is the typed jump-table decl of row 1140; this form deliberately leaves that as the named next step. I1 RESIDUAL (stated, not hidden): because exhaustiveness AND landing-point-correctness are the author's claim, the `@budget` ceiling passes SILENTLY if the corpus enumeration ever regresses to downstream labels (e.g. `targets(.drain_*)` instead of `targets(.slot_*)`) — the walk would measure the smaller number and vouch for a too-low ceiling. Inherent to the author's-claim design (the compiler checks existence/locality/distinctness/non-trailing, not that the author named the RIGHT landing points). The `targets_charge_the_landed_on_code_not_a_downstream_label` unit pin guards the mechanism; the corpus site is guarded only by review + the `ensure(DMA_CRITICAL_SLOTS==8)` message now naming the `.slot_k` labels. A prover-grade fix is the row-1140 typed table (the successor above).)

### CodeItem authorship (2026-08-05) — what the field carries but nothing consumes yet

- [codeitem-author, 2026-08-05] **`ItemAuthor::Splice { template }` is CARRIED, not consumed — the `-> Code` fn-contract parcel starts whole.** Every statement-call / bare-call / `{expr}` splice re-authors the template's own (`User`) lines as `Splice { template: <callee path> }`, so the stream now records WHICH template put each instruction there (nested templates keep the innermost name; compiler-authored items like an assert inside a template keep their finer fact). No lint reads it yet: the consumer is the t21 A1 ask above (**contract annotations on `-> Code` comptime fns** — declared clobbers/preserves on the fn's own surface), where `Splice`-authored writes get charged against the TEMPLATE's declared contract instead of the consumer proc, exactly as `Context`-authored SR traffic is charged to the context definition now. A language-round item; the author field just stopped the information loss. — OPEN
- [codeitem-author lens B, 2026-08-05] **Splice-authored SR traffic inside a context bracket is PROVEN at the definition but still CHARGED at every adopter.** A context whose acquire/release pulls SR traffic in via a NESTED splice (`acquire = asm { {sr_helper()} }` or a bare statement call inside the half) delivers items already `Splice{..}`-authored from the inner boundary; `splice_context_code` re-authors only `User` items, so they stay `Splice` — the definition-site round-trip check reads RANGES and passes, but `check_clobbers` exempts by AUTHOR and `Splice` is not exempt. Direction is a FALSE POSITIVE (proven-but-charged), never a soundness miss. Zero corpus instances (both live contexts are splice-free `asm {}` literals). Fix when demanded: lift `Splice` items to `Context` at the context boundary, or exempt `Splice`-inside-a-round-tripped-region. — OPEN (zero-instance)
- [codeitem-author lens B+C, 2026-08-05] **✅ CLOSED IN-PARCEL — the once-per-name check WAS an authored-but-unchecked path, and Lens C constructed it live.** Lens B flagged the shape abstractly (first-bracket-only checking vs comptime-varying halves); Lens C built the concrete constructor and DEMONSTRATED it end-to-end: a context whose halves reference a comptime fn's param (`if n == 1 { move.w sr,-(sp) }` …) evaluates per site in the consumer's env, so `gate(1)` round-trips (check passes, name inserted) and `gate(0)` splices a mask-with-no-restore that the once-per-name dedup never checks — `Context`-authored, exempt, ZERO diagnostics on a `clobbers()` proc that permanently masks interrupts. Exactly the §2-forbidden class. FIXED in-parcel per Lens C's disposition: the round-trip check runs at EVERY bracket over that site's actually-spliced ranges; only the REPORT is deduped (`sr_reported_contexts`). Regression pin: `lower_proc.rs::a_context_whose_halves_diverge_per_site_is_checked_at_every_site` (attack order, 1 firing at the definition). Corpus behavior identical (site-independent contexts round-trip at every site). — CLOSED
- [codeitem-author lens C, 2026-08-05] **A self-referential context crashes the compiler with a native stack overflow.** `context c { acquire = asm { with c { nop } } … }` plus one `with c` recurses `lower_with → splice_context_code → eval_asm → lower_with → …` without ever touching the comptime `call_stack`/step budget, so `MAX_CALL_DEPTH` never fires — `fatal runtime error: stack overflow, aborting`. Loud, not unsound (no wrong exemption ships), and pre-dates the author parcel (B′-1's splicer shape). Fix shape: a contexts-in-progress guard on the evaluator (push on `lower_with` entry, error `[context.recursive]` + lower the body bare on re-entry), with a unit test. Not taken in the author parcel (a new diagnostic id, outside its §5 no-new-diagnostics ruling). — OPEN
- [mul-lowering, 2026-08-05] **The corpus's every hand-derived multiply chain is WORD-width, and the ratified `mul_const` contract is the full 32-bit `mulu.w` result — so the design's own byte-identity acceptance bar (rediscover the ×66 chain) is unsatisfiable as ruled, and the sized variant the design §5 rejected now has measured demand.** Four sites: section.emp `Section_GetSecPtrXY` ×66 (`lsl.w #6` + `lsl.w #1` + `add.w`, 34 cycles), tile_cache.emp's DecompressBlock ×66 and `mul_cache_stride` ×80 (`lsl.w #6` + `lsl.w #4` + `add.w`), plane_buffer.emp ×160 (`lsl.w #7` + `lsl.w #5` + `add.w`) — each licensed by a module-level `ensure` bounding the product to a word. Under the long contract the model prices those chains WITH the mandatory zero-extend and every one ties-or-loses to `mulu` (66: 46 = 46 tie → mulu by bytes; 80: 48 vs 46; 160: 50 vs 46) — the hand chains win only BECAUSE they are word-width (×66 word chain = 34 cycles vs mulu's 46). A `mul_const.w` with the explicit contract "dst.w = (dst.w × n).w, upper word UNCHANGED-GARBAGE, author owns the range proof (the ensure idiom)" would adopt all four byte-identically and delete the re-derive-on-read burden the design names. Distinct NAME (the suffix), distinct contract — not the "two contracts for one name" trap §5 rejected. — CLOSED 2026-08-05 (specs/2026-08-05-mul-const-w-design.md ruled it UP; the mul-w parcel shipped `mul_const.w`/`mul_bounded.w` — word result, upper word UNDEFINED, author owns the range proof — and adopted all four stride sites byte-identically: ×66 34cy, ×80 40cy, ×160 44cy, each beating mulu's 46. See notes/2026-08-05-mul-w-packet.md §3 for the per-site cost derivation.)
- [mul-w, 2026-08-05] **The two repeated-add LOOP sites are NOT adopted (byte-changing, deferred).** section.emp `.gxy_mul` (`sec_y × grid_w`) and tile_cache.emp `.mul_loop` (`sec_y × grid_w`, cold path) are `mul_bounded.w` shapes, but the cost model picks `mulu` at any real bound (ceiling 70 vs loop 28 + 14·M word-width; loop wins only through M = 2) — so adopting `mul_bounded.w` there is byte-CHANGING, not byte-identical. It rides a deliberate byte-changing parcel with the pins/goldens re-freeze ripple (twin lockstep is gone post-K). NOTE the M ≤ 2 boundary is an ARTIFACT of the shared `moveq #0` accumulator seed, which is DEAD at word width (the loop's `add.w` never reads the accumulator's upper word): dropping that dead seed drops the setup to 24 + 14·M and flips M = 3 to loop-WINS (loop 66 vs mulu 70). So a future `mul_bounded.w` adoption parcel could pick the loop at real grid widths by shedding the seed — the adoption parcel decides, and the boundary quoted above is the current-generator boundary, not a fixed law. — CLOSED 2026-08-06 by the byte-changing parcel (specs/2026-08-06-byte-changing-mul-parcel.md R3+R4; notes/2026-08-06-ltr-mul-packet.md). BOTH loop sites adopted `mul_bounded.w dst, src, #MAX_ACT_SECTIONS` and the model chose **`mulu.w` at both** — the R4 "model-decides" exit, not a failure. The dead-seed prediction above was CONFIRMED and taken (R3 — precisely: the shed instruction is the MULTIPLICAND HOLDER's upper-word zero, not an accumulator seed; the accumulator's own `moveq #0, dst` remains.
