Setup 28 → 24, so M = 3 now loop-WINS and the boundary is mulu at M ≥ 4) — but the honest bound at these sites is `MAX_ACT_SECTIONS` = 48, far past the boundary, so mulu wins on worst case regardless of the seed. Worst case ~696 cy → 70 (≈ −600); the typical tiny-`sec_y` case pays ~+25 cy, recorded at each site as the construct's worst-case-vs-typical contract (both are documented cold paths). Side effect the parcel earned: `Section_GetSecPtrXY` no longer needs a d2 loop counter, so it PRESERVES d2 and its `Parallax_CheckBoundary` `[call.live-clobbered]` row left the D1c baseline. SCOPE CORRECTION (panel, spec §7 then §8): this row's two sites are closed, but the census that produced them was wrong THREE times over — `Section_FlatIDXY`'s `.fxy_mul`, `collision_lookup.emp`'s ×80 in `Collision_GetType` (hotter than anything this parcel touched), and `entity_window.emp`'s ×22 all went unseen; each stays open in its own row below. Any reading of this row as "the multiply sweep is complete" is false.
- [mul-lowering, 2026-08-05] **`mul_bounded`'s DEBUG `assert src ≤ M` is deferred — the bound is trusted in v1.** The design names "the existing assert.w idiom" but no compiler-emittable runtime-assert mechanism exists at the construct layer (engine-side release_fault is engine-owned), and a DEBUG-only emission would fork the expansion by build shape (the one-shape-per-invocation trap) while no adopter exists. Note the narrowness: a bound-violating src still computes the CORRECT product under either lowering (mulu is total; the loop iterates src times) — only the worst-case cost ceiling claim breaks. — OPEN (kill: first mul_bounded adopter in a budgeted proc, or a ratified compiler-assert mechanism)

- [z80-parity, 2026-08-05] **Z80 `ld a,i`/`ld a,r` (the flag-writing READ forms) are unassemblable today and must join the F-writer set the day they land.** The ISA `Mnemonic` enum (sigil-isa/z80.rs) has only `LdIA`/`LdRA` — `ld i,a`/`ld r,a`, which write `i`/`r`, not the flags (genuinely flag-neutral). The sibling `ld a,i`/`ld a,r` forms load `a` and set S/Z + copy IFF2 into P/V (they DO write `f`), but there is no `LdAI`/`LdAR` variant, so they cannot be encoded and cannot appear in a building corpus. `z80_flag_neutral` treats bare `ld` as neutral, which is correct only while these READ forms are unassemblable. — OPEN (kill: `LdAI`/`LdAR` (or any `ld a,i`/`ld a,r` read form) enters the ISA `Mnemonic` enum → they MUST leave the `ld`-is-neutral allowlist and enter the F-writer set, with a regression pin).
- [z80-parity, 2026-08-05] **Z80 has no condition-code vocabulary of its own — `VALID_CCS` accepts the 68k spellings regardless of CPU.** The Z80 `out(rN if cc)` flag rule (proc.rs check_out) and the out-verifier read the cc through the shared `VALID_CCS`, which lists 68k mnemonics (`eq`/`cs`/…); a Z80 proc writes `out(a if eq)`, not `out(a if z)`. Sound today (the cc is only ever tested for presence / rendered in messages, never decoded to a Z80 flag bit), but the spelling is a cross-CPU leak. — OPEN (deferred, row only; kill: a checker that must DECODE the cc to a specific Z80 flag, or a CPU-parametric `VALID_CCS` split — the corpus_contracts.rs:628 "when ccs go CPU-parametric" note is the same seam).

BUT recognizing `AbsSym` ALSO picks up the diagnostics desugars (`assert`/`raise_error`/`raise_exception` → `jsr (MDDBG__ErrorHandler).l` / `jmp (…_PagesController).l`), whose targets are contractless `pub equ … = extern("ErrorHandlerBlob")+off` LINK BOUNDARIES (neither proc nor extern proc). A recognized AbsSym callee resolves like a bare `Sym`; an equ/link boundary STAYS a hole (honest exit = an `extern proc` contract). Byte-neutral (analysis + tests only).

- [t-invoke, 2026-08-06] **The call-target extractor FAMILY — SIX spellings, one consolidated onto the AbsSym-aware form.** Six independent recognizers of "the symbol a call/tail names" exist: (1) `corpus_contracts.rs::call_target_sym` (the closure + `[context.unsatisfied]`), (2) `flag_check.rs::transfer_target_sym` (noreturn / computed-target / abs seam — the ONLY AbsSym-aware one, matches `Sym`+`SymOff`+`AbsSym`), (3) `preserves.rs::call_target`:1405 (the verified-`preserves` oracle), (4) `calls.rs::direct_target`:51 (D1b/D1c input/live-clobber), (5) `out_verify.rs::direct_target`:194 (the verified-out fixpoint), (6) `type_slice.rs::direct_target`:90 (G5 slot-type dispatch) — (3)-(6) are byte-identical `[CodeOperand::Sym(name)] if !name.contains('$')` matchers. Lane t-invoke consolidated (1) ONTO (2) so the closure reads `jsr (sym).l` (invoke edges); the matcher-family defect class (a shape one recognizer sees and another misses — 4th-family per the mnemonic-table lineage) shrinks by one. The four remaining bare-`Sym` matchers (3)-(6) are LEFT deliberately: widening any to AbsSym changes the conservative-but-sound behavior of the net it feeds (an abs-long call currently treated as opaque would start charging a callee's preserve-set / must-def / out-fixpoint / slot-type edge), so each is its own soundness-relevant parcel with its own gate run, not a mechanical rename. KILL CONDITION: this row closes when all four fold onto `transfer_target_sym` behind a measured re-run of the preserves oracle + D1b/D1c + out-verify + slot-type gates (byte-neutral corpus expected, but each net's residue re-confirmed). — OPEN (family unification; four of six spellings remain bare-`Sym`, each a scoped oracle-behavior change). AMENDED (Fable, 2026-08-06): line citations drifted — (3) is at `preserves.rs:1651` (not 1405), (6) at `type_slice.rs:104` (not 90) — and "(3)-(6) byte-identical" is WRONG: only (5)/(6) are identical; (4) puts the same sole-`Sym`+`$`-filter arm behind a CALL/TAIL mnemonic gate; (3) is `.iter().rev().find_map(Sym)` with NO `$`-hygiene filter, differing on two axes (last-Sym-of-any-arity vs sole-operand; unfiltered locals) before AbsSym is even considered — and `transfer_target_sym` itself is a rev-scan with no `$` filter, so folding (3) onto it and folding (5)/(6) onto it are DIFFERENT behavior changes. Lane X must scope each fold as its own multi-axis behavior question, not a mechanical rename. SECOND AMENDMENT (Fable, 2026-08-06, after Opus's counter-correction): the first amendment measured (3)'s axes against its SIBLINGS and left the ordering unstated — it had the decisive fact in hand (`transfer_target_sym` is itself a rev-scan with no `$` filter) and drew no conclusion from it. Measured against the fold TARGET, the ordering INVERTS: **(3) `preserves::call_target` is the CLEANEST fold — ONE axis** (operand kinds: it gains the `SymOff`/`AbsSym` arms; note a last-match scan that gains kinds can also change WHICH symbol wins on a mixed-kind multi-operand shape, not merely Some-vs-None, so even this fold gets a gate run). **(4)/(5)/(6) `direct_target` are the THREE-axis folds** (kinds + positional: exact sole-operand `[Sym]` vs last-match-of-any-arity + `$`-hygiene: each fold DROPS the `!name.contains('$')` filter, so `$`-mangled locals begin returning Some); (4) additionally carries its CALL/TAIL mnemonic gate, which is separable caller-side. The `$`-filter drop is its own standalone soundness question, answered PER CONSUMER: whether a mangled name reaching the consumer's map is a harmless lookup-miss or a live mis-credit depends on whether that net's map can ever hold a mangled key — verify per net, never assume the miss. Lane X WITHDRAWN from the 2026-08-06 round's dispatch (Opus): there is no "easy three"; re-scope before any porter picks it up.

- [trackc, 2026-08-06] **The niche-option INLINE form (`u8 where LO..HI ? S`) registers no payload, so `[option.unguarded-use]` is structurally incapable of firing on options spelled that way.** `collect_option_payloads` maps an option to its payload only when the underlying is a NAMED newtype (`newtype_of` returns `None` for a prim), which is exactly what the inline form produces. Both forms are supported and tested (`inline_where_then_sentinel_parses_and_resolves`), and the inline form is fine for a niche declared purely for `.none` + `[option.raw-sentinel]` — but an option whose values are meant to be REFUSED at typed call slots must use the two-decl payload/option pair. Options: teach `collect_option_payloads` to synthesize an anonymous payload identity for prim-underlying options, or reject the inline form at a typed slot — neither has a customer yet, so this is recorded, not built.

Both are fixed, but the NEXT marker repeats the hunt. A first-class marker kind (emits nothing, costs nothing, writes nothing, CC-transparent by construction) would make the default correct and the opt-out explicit. Related: `flag_check::writes_carry` (an allowlist) and `cc_transparent` (deny-by-default) already disagree about the same mnemonic class by design, which is what made the omission invisible.
- [trackc lens panel, 2026-08-06] **There is no lint-id REGISTRY — ids are bare strings formatted at each emission site, so a typo'd or duplicated id is invisible and no gate can enumerate the tier of a new lint.** Adding three ids this parcel (`[option.niche-overlap]`, `[option.unguarded-use]`, `[option.raw-sentinel]`) required nothing but writing them into `format!` strings; nothing checks that a warn-tier id is listed in `warn_tier_corpus`'s baseline vocabulary, that an id is spelled the same at two sites, or that the docs' triage table covers it. A central registry (id → tier → doc anchor) would make the warn-tier baseline and the triage note derivable instead of hand-maintained.
- [pw lens panel, 2026-08-06] **The POP and PEEK restore arms model the same event twice, and the asymmetry hid a soundness hole.** `preserves.rs`' pop arm drains slots and checks byte balance (`got != want` → bail); the `(sp)` peek arm reads `st.stack.last()` and credits directly, with NO balance check. So the width discipline the pop arm gets structurally, the peek arm had to get from the credit function — and until this parcel's fixup it did not: `move.l d5,-(sp)` / `move.w (sp),d5` credited `preserves(d5.w)` while the machine loaded the saved HIGH word into d5's low word (big-endian). Fixed by requiring `restore_w == save_w` in `credit_restore`, which both arms now share. THE ASK: one `restore_slot(st, reg, slot, width)` primitive that owns drain-or-peek, width agreement, and facet credit, so the next facet (a `.b`, or a second register file) cannot land on one arm and miss the other. Found independently by lenses B and C, which is itself the signal — two readers reconstructing the same missing invariant from opposite ends. — RECORDED
- [pw lens panel, 2026-08-06] **A contract TYPE's `preserves` clause is never validated, so a facet typo in one is silent.** `check_preserves` runs over `ProcDecl`; `contract_type_bound` reads `type X = proc (...) preserves(...)` through `expand_reglist_regs`, which DROPS any token it does not recognise. `ObjRoutine`'s narrowing to `preserves(a0, d7.w)` this parcel is therefore correct-by-luck in the safe direction (a dropped token makes the bound WIDER — more clobbered), but `preserves(a0, d7.q)` would drop just as silently and read as "preserves a0 only", with no diagnostic. The proc-side validator arms (`[proc.preserves-invalid]`) have no type-side counterpart. Cheap to close: run the same `WordFacet::fold_token` + register-spelling validation over every contract type's clauses at collection time. — RECORDED

- [mul2, 2026-08-06] **LIVE SILENT-WRONG-ANSWER BUG, and it is the SECOND instance of a class this ledger already barred: `EntityWindow_MigrateMasks` strides `Entity_Scan_State` by 22 where the struct is 26.** `struct EntityScanState (size: $1A)` = 26 bytes, `ENTITY_SCAN_STATE_LEN = $1A` in `engine/ram.emp:37`, and every other indexing site in `engine/objects/entity_window.emp` uses `sizeof(EntityScanState)` (`:603`, `:789`, `:819`, `:926`, `:1350`, `:1452-1458`, `:1548-1554`, `:1678-1681`, `:1746` — the last two re-resolved by reading the file this session; they were cited 6 lines short). The `.new_loop` head of `EntityWindow_MigrateMasks` instead spells the stride as a hand shift chain — `move.w d3,d0 / lsl.w #4,d0 / move.w d3,d1 / lsl.w #2,d1 / add.w d1,d0 / move.w d3,d1 / add.w d1,d1 / add.w d1,d0` = **×22** — and then reads `Entity_Scan_State + d0 + offsetof(ess_section_id)`. Entry 0 is correct; entry 1 reads offset 40 instead of 44, entry 2 reads 62 instead of 70, entry 3 reads 84 instead of 96 — i.e. bytes inside `ess_rom_type_tbl_ptr`, `ess_rom_obj_ptr` and `ess_rom_ring_ptr` respectively, ROM-pointer bytes read as section ids. All stay inside the 104-byte array, so there is no out-of-bounds access and no crash. **The consequence is sharper than "masks are not migrated", and it runs in BOTH directions — the follow-up parcel's A/B must be designed to exhibit each.** `EntityWindow_InitSection` (`engine/objects/entity_window.emp:630`, re-cited this session; the mask clear is the `cmp.b EntityScanState.ess_section_id(a1), d1 / beq .same_section` block at `:634-646`) zeroes an entry's loaded mask ONLY when that entry's section changed, and `EntityWindow_Slide` runs `BuildEntries` (hence `InitSection`) BEFORE `MigrateMasks`. So: **(a)** an entry that CHANGED section has a zeroed mask and then fails the identity match on a garbage id, so its surviving section's already-spawned entities are re-treated as unloaded — **duplicate spawns**, not merely un-migrated masks; **(b)** an entry that KEPT its section has a correct, non-zeroed mask, and `MigrateMasks` still runs its loop over it — a garbage byte that chance-matches an old snapshot id sends control to `.match`, which copies a FOREIGN 32-byte mask over a correct one, corrupting an entry that needed no migration at all. Same family as the "wrong mask migration on window slides" symptom the first instance produced, with the duplicate-spawn direction newly identified. **PROVENANCE, git-verified:** commit `4ad9f9b` (2026-07-31, "entity_window #1 — per-section trigger cache") grew the struct `$16 -> $1A` for the two new trigger-cache fields and updated every `sizeof`/`offsetof` site and both `ensure`s — and did not touch this chain, which was CORRECT at ×22 the moment before. `git show 4ad9f9b~1` and `git show 4ad9f9b` show the eight-instruction block byte-for-byte unchanged across that commit. **THIS IS A RECURRENCE OF THE ROW ABOVE AT LINE ~1349** ("Hand-computed struct strides are a BYTE-GATE BLIND SPOT", 2026-07-22), where the same proc, the same array and the same chain broke in the opposite direction ($1A -> $16 left it at ×26). That row's BAR — "any struct field-count/size change greps the module for hand-computed strides (shift decompositions, `mulu #k`, literal `#stride`), not only symbolic `sizeof`/`offsetof`" — was in the ledger and was not applied nine days later. The byte gate is structurally blind to it (the wrong stride is what both the golden and the fresh build agree on); boot is blind to it (wrong mask is not a crash); and the stale-comment tell that caught it the first time was present again, twice: `:48` said the records are "$16 bytes each" and the chain's own comment said "entry stride $16", both false since 4ad9f9b. **Both are CORRECTED in the mul2 fixup round, together with a KNOWN-BUG marker at the chain naming this row** — the aeon repo previously carried no record of the defect at all, only the two false comments, so a reader there could re-derive ×22 as intentional. **NOT FIXED HERE, and Site B's planned `mul_const.w` adoption was REFUSED because of it** — adopting `mul_const.w d0, #22, d1` would launder a wrong constant into a construct call that reads as deliberate, and the fix is a BEHAVIOUR change that must not ride inside a byte-proof optimization parcel whose A/B is an identity check. The fix belongs in its own parcel with an A/B designed to EXHIBIT the difference (slide the window across a section boundary with entities already collected in a surviving section, and watch the masks migrate). **Spell the fix as `mul_const.w d0, #sizeof(EntityScanState), d1` if the construct accepts that immediate** — that both adopts the construct and extinguishes the class at this site permanently, since the multiplier then derives from the struct instead of restating it. A bare named comptime `const` is measured-accepted (row below); `sizeof(...)` specifically is NOT, so verify it before committing to the spelling, and if it is refused, fix the chain to ×26 and retain an `ensure(sizeof(EntityScanState) == 26, ...)` beside it. The two stale `$16` comments and the KNOWN-BUG marker are already in the tree; the parcel deletes the marker when it deletes the bug. **[migmask, 2026-08-06] The marker is now GONE on the `migmask` branch** — the stride derives from the struct and the marker was replaced by a present-tense contract comment there. **[migmask A/B, 2026-08-06] THE A/B HAS NOW BEEN EXECUTED, and the mechanism is PROVEN LIVE on the shipping code path.** Probe 1 hit 8 of 8 predictions exactly — `d0.w` reads `$0000/$0016/$002C/$0042` on the OLD cart and `$0000/$001A/$0034/$004E` on the NEW — and the overseer added corroboration nobody specified: because the breakpoint sits ON the `move.b`, `d2` still holds the previously fetched id, and reading it across the hits gives OLD `$01, $1F, $1F` (a repeated ROM-pointer byte) against NEW `$01, $02, $04` (a coherent set of section ids for a 2×2 window). That is the defect exhibited directly, not inferred. Probe 3 is answered (row below: the bug was present in the recording build but never fired there). **Probe 2 — the mask-outcome comparison — is OWED**, and its first run is not a refutation: the capture was taken at the next `EntityWindow_Scan`, which is DOWNSTREAM of the post-slide entity re-load that repopulates the masks (post-slide slot 1 read `$03` on both carts, a value that was neither input — rebuilt, not migrated), and the camera poke was applied at an arbitrary pause where `Camera_Update` pulled it back (`$0C000000` → `$0BF00000`) so the slide fired only on some runs. Both are executor deviations from this spec, which already said to capture at `MigrateMasks`' RETURN and to apply the poke AT the `EntityWindow_Scan` breakpoint (`$0046A8`, downstream of `Camera_Update`) — and the second was confirmed by the overseer to fire reliably on both carts when done that way. **[migmask probe 2, 2026-08-06 second sitting] PROBE 2 IS NOW ESTABLISHED AND THE ROW IS CLOSED.** Re-run per the spec — poke at the `EntityWindow_Scan` breakpoint (`$0046A8`, downstream of `Camera_Update`), capture by `step_out` at `MigrateMasks`' RETURN. The drive is a scripted camera walk (LIVE input, no buttons, `Logic_Tick`-anchored), because probe 3 proved the fixture never slides. **The exhibit needs a populated section that survives onto a MISREAD entry, and only a LEFTWARD slide provides one** — entry 0 is `0 × stride` and is correct under both strides, so from the boot anchor no rightward or downward slide can differ; the schedule walks right to populate section 1, slides right to seat it on entry 0, then slides left to move it to entry 1. **Control, measured not assumed:** both carts were byte-identical at every checkpoint up to the leftward slide, including through the rightward slide (whose only survivor lands on entry 0 — the direction model predicts no difference there, and none occurred), and at the leftward slide's `MigrateMasks` entry the proc's whole input matched byte for byte, `a4` = `$FFFFAD46` and in fact the entire register file. **Content precondition satisfied at k=1:** id `$01`, present in the snapshot ids (`$01 $02 $04 $05`) at j=0, block non-zero (`$3F` rings + `$01` obj); the ×22 read lands on `$1F`, a `ess_rom_*_ptr` byte absent from the snapshot set. **OUTPUT at the proc's return: OLD all 128 bytes ZERO (the mask is LOST); NEW slot 1 = `$3F` + obj `$01` (MIGRATED). Exactly one slot differs, slot 1 — the predicted count.** That is direction (a). **CORROBORATION, and it is the engine's own tripwire:** with the leftward poke at `$09FF` so the rings stay in band and are re-scanned, the OLD cart raises `assert.w d5,ne,d4`, `Got: 0100`, at `$47BA` inside `EntityWindow_TrySpawnRing` — the DEBUG no-dup scan whose source comment reads "always fails: duplicate (sec,idx)" — with the key word naming section `$01`, list index `$00`, exactly the section whose mask was lost. The NEW cart runs the identical schedule with no assertion. So the duplicate spawn is not inferred from mask bytes; the engine detects it on the buggy cart and stays silent on the fixed one. Screenshots in `notes/assets/2026-08-06-migmask-probe2-{old-dup-assert,new-clean}.png`. — **CLOSED (mechanism live by probe 1, consequence established by probe 2 in both the mask outcome and the downstream symptom, fixture settled by probe 3).**
- [mul2, 2026-08-06] **A construct adoption is a stronger fix for the hand-stride class than the BAR the class already has — and this parcel is the evidence for both halves.** The 2026-07-22 row asked future authors to REMEMBER to grep for shift decompositions after a struct-size change; nine days later they did not, and the same proc broke again. A `mul_const.w dst, #sizeof(T), scratch` spelling needs no one to remember anything: the multiplier is derived, so a struct-size change updates it or fails the build. The general shape of the ask is that **every hand-decomposed constant multiply in the corpus is a latent instance of this class whenever its constant is a struct size, an entry stride or an array pitch** — the multiply construct's real value is not the 8 cycles it saved at `Collision_GetType`, it is that a construct call can carry a derived constant where a shift chain can only carry a literal. A census of hand chains whose constant is a *layout-derived* number would size the remaining exposure. **The ×80 collision stride is NOT the counter-example this row originally offered it as:** `TILE_CACHE_COLL_SIZE = TILE_CACHE_COLS * TILE_CACHE_COLL_ROWS`, so the collision pitch is an ARRAY PITCH — layout-derived, squarely inside the class, not an algebraic constant outside it. Nor is it uniformly guarded: after the mul2 fixup revert, two `ensure(TILE_CACHE_STRIDE == 80, ...)` guards exist and cover 2 of the 8 stride-derived multiply sites counted this session (see the deferred stride-naming row below); the other 6 carry no guard at all. Demand census: 1 confirmed live bug, 1 prior instance, corpus-wide count unmeasured. — **OPEN (kill: a census of layout-derived hand multiplies is run and each is either adopted with a derived immediate or guarded by an `ensure`)**

Re-running the identical command with the streams SEPARATED (`> out 2> err`) gave the true **3502 / 0 / 4 = 3506**, closing exactly. The tell that the first capture was corrupt rather than genuinely surplus: summing the per-binary `running N tests` headers gave 3506 while summing the `test result:` lines gave 3520 — **two independent totals from the same file disagreeing is the signature**, and the `running N` sum is the one to trust because it is emitted before any interleaving window opens. **BAR: capture strict with `> out 2> err`, and cross-check the `running N tests` sum against the `test result:` sum before quoting either.** — RECORDED

  2. **It named the WRONG constant at the collision site.** `TILE_CACHE_STRIDE = TILE_CACHE_COLS` (`engine/system/constants.emp:215`) is an ALIAS whose stated meaning is the *nametable* row stride.   · *Nametable row stride (`#TILE_CACHE_STRIDE`, five):* `tile_cache.emp:78` (the `mul_cache_stride` comptime fn, expanded at `:101` and `:1365`) · `tile_cache.emp` the hand chain at `:328-331` (`move.w d2,d5 / lsl.w #2,d5 / add.w d2,d5 / lsl.w #4,d5`) · `plane_buffer.emp:244` · `plane_buffer.emp:76` (×160) · `section.emp:277` (×160).

  **TWO OPEN QUESTIONS THE PARCEL MUST SETTLE, neither answered by mul2's measurement:** (a) whether the immediate accepts an EXPRESSION such as `#(TILE_CACHE_STRIDE*2)` — mul2 measured a bare named `const` only, and the two ×160 sites need an expression or a second named constant; (b) `sizeof(...)` as an immediate, which the MigrateMasks fix also needs.

A construct is not the fix there; a retained guard is. So the end state is not "all eight adopt" — it is *six adopt with the right constant per site, and at least one `ensure` survives to cover the two that cannot.* — **OPEN (kill: a dedicated parcel adopts the six construct-spelled sites with `#TILE_CACHE_COLS` at the three collision sites and `#TILE_CACHE_STRIDE`/its ×2 form at the five nametable sites, retains a guard covering the two hand chains, and re-proves the seven-target byte bar)**

Measured this session by instrumenting that arm and rebuilding every shape: **`player_common.emp:286 `bne Player_DebugMove`** (inside `Player_Main`, `preserves(d7.w)`) and **`games/sonic4/objects/test_player.emp:129 `bne TestPlayer_Debug`** (inside `TestPlayer_Main`, `preserves(a0, d7.w)`). Observation counts are SHAPE-SCOPED, which the first draft of this row did not say: plain s4 / config_b / lean = **5 observations, one site** (`Player_DebugMove`, 1 Full-facet pass + 4 Word); s4.debug / config_a = **14 observations, both sites** (`Player_DebugMove` 5, `TestPlayer_Debug` 9 — nearly double, because `preserves(a0, d7.w)` drives both the Full and the Word facet where `preserves(d7.w)` drives only the Word); demo / demo.debug = **0**. Against 39 `TailOut` (plain) / 108 (debug). **NO verdict depends on the marking at either site, and no false negative is demonstrable at either.** Three independent reasons, each verified: (1) `preserves.rs:381-383`'s `if has_call { ever_clobbered = [true; 16]; }` runs BEFORE the marking, and `has_call` measured TRUE at 100% of observations (both procs contain `jbsr`), so every bit is already set and the marking cannot flip one; (2) `ever_clobbered` is consumed at exactly ONE place — the `bailed_reached_exit && clobbered` arm — where a set bit can only turn a verdict INTO `Unverifiable`, i.e. into a refusal, never into a credit; (3) whether the callee oracle would even mark the checked register is policy-dependent (measured: under `CallPolicy::ClobberAll` all 15 registers mark, under `PreserveAll` none do), so the "`Player_DebugMove` declares `clobbers(d0-d3/a1)` and d7 is not among them" argument does NOT hold in general — it is reason (1) that is load-bearing. The widening (`Edge::TailOut | Edge::BranchOut`, identical body) is therefore correct because OVER-obligation is that arm's safe direction, and a `TailOut`-only widening would have been a silent narrowing with no measurable effect today. Byte-neutral ×7. Recorded so the census is neither re-cited as "`BranchOut` has no corpus witness" nor as "a false negative was averted". — RECORDED (measurement, no defect).

After: the leg exists, the edges are `[BranchOut, Follow(ft)]`, `divergent_terminal` is true (the target is `@noreturn`), the first arm of the structural loop swallows the leaving edge, and the budget is CHARGED and can PASS. A refusal became a number, created purely by the edge's existence — exactly the "restored edge turns a refusal into a credit" direction this review hunted. It is SOUND: the taken leg diverges, so the path genuinely ends there, and it is charged the taken cost with `succ: None` — the same treatment an `@noreturn` unconditional tail already gets, and the fall-through leg is still walked and still charged. - [lane E, 2026-08-06] **`out_verify`'s `BranchOut` skip is a KNOWN INCOMPLETENESS in the module's own dangerous polarity, and the parcel gave it its only exemplar.** A conditional branch out of the body is skipped rather than charged as a required return path, so a proc that leaves via `bne ErrorHandler` with an `out(rN)` register unproduced is still blessed `Produced`, and every caller then credits rN as defined on a path where it is not — an `out` UNDER-report, which is the direction that breaks callers. - [migmask, 2026-08-06] **`sizeof(...)` IS accepted as a `mul_const` immediate — measured, and the derivation is PINNED both polarities. This closes sub-question (b) of the stride-naming row.** The mul2 fixup panel left two open language questions; this is the one the `EntityWindow_MigrateMasks` fix needed, and it was a measured-unknown, not a prediction. Measured this session by building the corpus: `mul_const.w d0, #sizeof(EntityScanState), d1` compiles, and the mechanism is ordinary — the immediate operand mapper evaluates the expression and folds it through `Value::as_stored_int` to `CodeOperand::Imm` (`eval/asm.rs:1748-1749`), so any comptime integer expression reaches the multiplier, not just a bare named `const`. **The lowering it selects for 26 is a 6-instruction LTR chain** — `move.w d0,d1 / lsl.w #1,d0 / add.w d1,d0 / lsl.w #2,d0 / add.w d1,d0 / lsl.w #1,d0` = 12 B / 38 cy, chosen over `mulu.w #26,d0` (4 B / **48 cy**) on the cost model, and 2 B / 6 cy cheaper than the 7-instruction hand chain it replaced. **The 48 is re-derived against the table, correcting a first-draft "~70" that was wrong by 22 cycles:** `sigil-isa/src/m68k_cycles.rs:303-305` prices a KNOWN-IMMEDIATE `mulu.w` exactly as `38 + 2·ones(n) + ea_time(Imm)`, and `ones(26) = 3` (`%11010`) with a 4-cycle word immediate fetch gives `38 + 6 + 4 = 48`. 70 is the *ceiling for the register-source form* (the `at_most(70 + ea)` row immediately below), which is not the candidate that was rejected. The decision is unaffected (38 < 48) but the margin was recorded as a measurement, so the wrong number mattered. **The scratch is load-bearing for the chain:** measured from the lowering oracle's own output, the scratch-free `.w` candidate set for 26 picks `mulu.w #26,d0` — the 6-instruction chain exists only because the site can grant `d1`. Pinned by `crates/sigil-frontend-emp/tests/mul_lowering.rs::a_sizeof_multiplier_derives_from_the_struct`, which asserts BOTH polarities plus the non-vacuity guard the measurement actually needs: `sizeof` of a 26-byte struct is byte-identical to `#26`, `sizeof` of the same struct shrunk to 22 bytes is byte-identical to `#22`, and the two differ from each other. Without that third assertion a spelling that compiled and silently ignored the struct would pass. **What this does NOT answer:** the stride-naming row's OTHER sub-question, whether an EXPRESSION like `#(TILE_CACHE_STRIDE*2)` is accepted, is still unmeasured — the mechanism above says it should be, but "should be" is not a measurement and the two ×160 sites depend on it. — **CLOSED for the `sizeof` half; the expression half stays open in the stride-naming row.**

(A first draft said "222 B in the three release shapes, 320 B in the three debug shapes", which accounts for only 6 of 7, and "nine" for all shapes. The partition is exactly non-DEBUG vs DEBUG — a first-draft packet disclaimer that it tracked "shapes carrying extra DEBUG code, not the release/debug label" was false and is withdrawn.) **Nothing else in any ROM changed**: `full_size` is identical for all seven targets, `anchor_end` (EndOfRom) is identical for all seven, `repin` reported `pins.rs unchanged`, and the seven `golden/offcanonical_sizes/*.txt` tables changed ONLY in their two CRC header lines — not one of the 62/74/41/43/78/60/62 boundary labels moved. **THE MECHANISM IS A FIXED PLACEMENT SLOT, NOT PRE-EXISTING SLACK** — a first draft of this row said "the module's own slot had ≥2 bytes of tail padding, so the shrink was consumed", and the measurement refutes that framing. Three of the seven had ZERO padding beforehand, so nothing absorbed anything there; in all seven the pad grew by exactly 2 because the next section head sits at a fixed org (`PopulateSpawnedPieceCount`, `$3F00` plain / `$4C60` debug), so the freed bytes BECOME padding rather than being consumed by it. Recorded because the campaign's default assumption for a size-changing parcel is a relocation ladder plus a pins ripple, and here the correct answer was neither — the ripple surface to hand-check was empty, and the proof of that is the size tables, not an assertion. — RECORDED (measurement).
- [migmask, 2026-08-06] **THE SHIPPED OJZ REPLAY FIXTURE WAS RECORDED FROM A BUILD CARRYING THE BUG — AND THE BUG NEVER FIRED IN IT. Measured, not reasoned; the row's original headline ("a recording of the bug") was the reasonable inference and it was WRONG.** `games/sonic4/data/replays/ojz_fixture.bin` is a 2059-tick input stream with 33 curated state checkpoints; in DEBUG a mismatch raises `REPLAY DESYNC` (`engine/system/replay.emp`), and the curated hash covers SST (object) state. **Git-verified dating:** the fixture's last content-changing commit is aeon `806a0de` (2026-08-05), and the stride bug was introduced by `4ad9f9b` (2026-07-31) — so every checkpoint hash in the shipped fixture was captured with `MigrateMasks` mis-indexing, i.e. with duplicate spawns and/or foreign-mask copies already in the state being hashed. If the stream crosses a section boundary with entities collected in a surviving section, the FIXED ROM must desync against its own regression net. This is invisible to every gate the porter can run: the fixture is played only on the emulator, and `git grep` over `crates/*/tests` and `crates/*/src` finds no consumer of it — the strict suite never touches it. **Both outcomes are informative** and the A/B note specifies the run with its non-vacuity control (OLD must first reach `Replay_Done = $FF` with zero desyncs, which is what `b014865` recorded; if OLD desyncs too, the fixture is stale for an unrelated reason and the probe proves nothing): a desync is the sharpest available exhibit that shipped behaviour changed, and a clean run bounds the fix's blast radius. **THE RUN, executed by the overseer 2026-08-06 (A/B note, probe 3):** both carts ran the fixture from the anchored init breakpoint to completion. `Replay_Ptr` ended at `$0005E6A0` on both, `Replay_Done` = `$FF` on both, zero desyncs on both, and `Entity_Loaded_Masks` after the full 2059 ticks hashed **`0x25913C7E` identically on both carts**. The reason is sharper than "no qualifying slide": **the id-read breakpoint was never hit at all — the window never slides in this stream.** The anchor stays `(0,0)` and the camera never leaves section 0, so `EntityWindow_MigrateMasks` never executes. **Therefore the 33 checkpoint hashes do NOT encode the mis-indexing, the fixture does NOT need re-recording on account of this parcel, and it will not desync against the fix.** The dating argument established that the bug was PRESENT in the recording build; only the run establishes that it never FIRED, and those are different claims — which is precisely the distinction the fixup round forced into the packet's prose, one round before the run proved it load-bearing. **The general defect still stands as a class** (a recorded regression net captured from a buggy build silently promotes the bug to the spec, and re-recording is the only repair) — it simply did not happen here, and "reason about the dating, then MEASURE whether it fired" is the procedure the class needs. **Second-order finding, worth more than the row's original claim: the shipped deterministic regression stream never exercises `MigrateMasks` at all**, so the corpus's only automated behavioural net has ZERO coverage of the entity-window slide path — the bug survived nine days partly because nothing that runs could have caught it. — **CLOSED (measured: present in the recording build, never fired in it, no re-record owed). The coverage gap it exposed is carried as its own row below.**
- [migmask A/B, 2026-08-06] **THE SHIPPED REPLAY FIXTURE HAS ZERO COVERAGE OF THE ENTITY-WINDOW SLIDE PATH — the 2059-tick stream never leaves section 0.** Measured while executing probe 3 (row above): the id-read breakpoint inside `EntityWindow_MigrateMasks` was never hit across the whole fixture, `Entity_Window_Anchor` stayed `(0,0)`, and the post-run `Entity_Loaded_Masks` hash was identical on the buggy and fixed carts. So `EntityWindow_Slide`, `EntityWindow_MigrateMasks`, `EntityWindow_PopulateSectionRings` and the compare-clear in `EntityWindow_InitSection` are all outside the corpus's only automated behavioural net. **This is why the ×22 stride survived nine days and two byte-changing parcels:** the byte gate is structurally blind to a wrong constant (golden and fresh build agree on it), boot is blind to it (a wrong mask is not a crash), and the replay net never reaches the code. A stream that traverses at least one section boundary in each of the four directions with rings collected in a surviving section would cover the class; recording one needs `INPUT_RECORD` (DEBUG-only, already in the tree, procedure note in aeon `b014865`) and is a bounded, deterministic job. — **OPEN (kill: a second recorded fixture that crosses section boundaries ships alongside `ojz_fixture.bin`, and its checkpoints are proven to move when `MigrateMasks` is broken — i.e. it fails on the OLD cart).**
- [migmask, 2026-08-06] **The 07-22 bar applied as a SWEEP over `engine/objects/entity_window.emp`: the ×22 chain was the only unguarded restated stride, and the remaining restatements are shift counts, which have no derived spelling.** The bar ("any struct field-count/size change greps the module for hand-computed strides") was run this session over the whole module — every `lsl`/`lsr`/`asl`/`asr`/`mul*` — rather than only over the site the brief named. **The enumeration below was RE-COUNTED line by line in the fixup round; the first pass's list was incomplete in three places and its conclusion, while independently confirmed by two later re-censuses, was not established by the evidence it offered. Said plainly rather than quietly repaired.** The complete census — 23 shift/multiply sites in the module, every one placed: (a) the ×22 chain, fixed — the only unguarded restated STRUCT stride; (b) SIX `lsl.w #5` sites (`:545/:561/:579/:641/:1642/:1646`, re-resolved against the POST-fix file) restating `log2(ENTITY_LOADED_SLOT_SIZE)` — these ARE the same restatement class, but they are covered by a live tripwire, `ensure(ENTITY_LOADED_SLOT_SIZE == 32, "EntityLoaded primitives assume 32-byte slots (lsl #5)")` at `:93`, so a size change fails the build loudly instead of silently; (c) `lsl.w #2` at `:1071` and `:1236` are ROM-table entry strides (4-byte `dc.w X, dc.w Y` ring entries; 4-byte longword type-table entries), documented at both sites and not derived from any struct; (d) `lsr.w #3` **×7** (`:188/:213/:235/:264/:550/:566/:584` — the first pass said ×4) is bit-index→byte-offset, `lsl.w #8` at `:1015` is a byte-to-high-half placement, and `lsr.w #OEF_TYPE_SHIFT` at `:1233` is a named bitfield extract — none is a stride; (e) EIGHT register shifts by `SECTION_SIZE_SHIFT` (`:691/:697/:729/:731/:837/:840/:1693/:1696`), guarded by `ensure(SECTION_SIZE == 1 << SECTION_SIZE_SHIFT, …)` at `:97`; (f) **`lsr.w #1` at `:748`, which the first pass filed in NO bucket at all.** With `andi.w #1, d0` at `:745` it decodes an entry index into (col, row) — an unguarded restatement of `MAX_TRACKED_SECTIONS == 4` *being a 2×2 window*. The module's only guard on that constant is `ensure(MAX_TRACKED_SECTIONS <= 16, …)` at `:45`, written for an unrelated reason (a `btst` bit index), and it admits 6, 8, 9 and 16 — every one of which silently breaks the decode into garbage geometry. **Disposition: ledgered, not fixed here** — it is not a struct stride, so it is outside this parcel's class and outside its byte proof, and unlike the ×22 chain the correct spelling is not obvious (the window is 2×2 by the envelope argument in `DeriveWindow`'s header, not by arithmetic on `MAX_TRACKED_SECTIONS`); the cheap honest repair is a second `ensure(MAX_TRACKED_SECTIONS == 4, …)` naming the 2×2 decode, and it belongs with the next parcel that touches the window geometry. Cross-module: `Entity_Scan_State` has exactly one other consumer, `engine/ram.emp:37-39`, and it already carries `ensure(extern("EntityScanState_len") == ENTITY_SCAN_STATE_LEN, …)`. **The gap the sweep exposes is (b)'s shape, not (b)'s safety: a shift count cannot DERIVE from a size.** `mul_const.w #sizeof(...)` is the wrong instrument for a power of two (12 B / 38 cy against a 2-byte `lsl`), the shift-count position DOES accept comptime expressions (`camera.emp:141` spells `#SECTION_SIZE_SHIFT-8`), and there is no `log2` — the builtin table (`eval/builtins.rs`) holds `map filter fold len find slice val range array byte dense pad_to_cycles nop jr extern bankid winptr` and nothing arithmetic. So the corpus's answer is a hand-maintained `*_SHIFT` beside a `*_SIZE` — a second constant that can drift from the first. **The exemplar list was re-checked in the fixup round and only ONE of the four first-draft members survives:** `BLOCK_TILE_SHIFT` = 4 / `BLOCK_TILE_SIZE` = 16 (`engine/system/constants.emp:440-441`) is a genuine pair and is **UNGUARDED** — no `ensure` anywhere relates them, and `BLOCK_TILE_SHIFT` drives 10 shift sites in `tile_cache.emp` (`:56/:59/:511/:515/:519/:523/:534/:537/:553/:555`). **That is this row's real find.** The other three were wrong: `SECTION_SIZE_SHIFT` IS guarded (`ensure(SECTION_SIZE == 1 << SECTION_SIZE_SHIFT, …)`, `entity_window.emp:97`, plus a range `ensure` at `camera.emp:19`) and is not a gap; `PARALLAX_LERP_SHIFT` = 4 is a lerp RATE with no `*_SIZE` partner and could not have one — **not a member, dropped**; `TILE_BYTES_SHIFT` = 5 (`bg_anim.emp:76`) has no `*_SIZE` partner either, which makes it **a different defect — a shift with no size at all**, the 32-bytes-per-8×8-tile fact existing only in a comment, and it is re-filed as that rather than as a drift-prone pair. A `log2()` comptime builtin (refusing non-powers-of-two, which is itself the check the `ensure`s hand-write) would let `lsl.w #log2(ENTITY_LOADED_SLOT_SIZE), d0` derive the same way the multiplier now does, and would retire the paired-constant idiom corpus-wide. — **OPEN (kill: a `log2()` comptime builtin lands with its non-power-of-two refusal, and the `*_SHIFT`/`*_SIZE` pairs collapse; or a ruling states the paired constant plus its `ensure` is the permanent idiom).**

What is genuinely new is the FILE scope: the two surviving `ensure(TILE_CACHE_STRIDE == 80, …)` tripwires live in `tile_cache.emp:76` and `collision_lookup.emp:28`, so a reader inside `plane_buffer.emp` has no in-file signal that `#80`/`#160` are derived numbers at all. Contrast the closest analogue to the ×22 bug — a STRUCT size restated as a literal — which IS guarded at both its sites: `mul_const.w #66` at `section.emp:151` and `tile_cache.emp:233`, each under an `ensure(sizeof(Sec) == 66, …)` in its own file. Now that `sizeof(...)` is measured-accepted as an immediate (row above), both `#66` sites could spell `#sizeof(Sec)` and retire their guards honestly; the `#80`/`#160` sites cannot (the stride is a config constant, not a struct size) and must keep a guard. — RECORDED (cross-reference; the stride-naming row owns the kill condition, and its adoption parcel should add an `ensure` to `plane_buffer.emp` whether or not it migrates the spelling).
- [migmask fixup, 2026-08-06] **An A/B SPECIFICATION needs the same adversarial review as code, and this one carried three defects that would each have wasted a live run — two of them producing a WRONG conclusion.** The shipped code change was sound (a lens decoded it from the golden bytes and refuted two independent failure hypotheses); every finding was in the note the overseer would execute. (1) **The stated drive removed the proc under test.** The draft fell back on `Debug_Scene_Freeze` + a camera poke, but `ojz_scroll_test.emp:256-263` gates `jbsr EntityWindow_Scan` behind exactly that flag, and `Scan` is the sole caller of `Slide`, the sole caller of `MigrateMasks`. The run would have reported "no difference" while executing nothing. (2) **The non-vacuity control contradicted the instruction, and its inevitable failure was pre-written as a refutation.** Step 1 required the proc's inputs byte-identical across carts; step 3 said "run several consecutive slides". Once slide N writes different masks, slide N+1's inputs differ BY CONSTRUCTION — that divergence IS the fix working — yet the draft's "what would refute the parcel" section listed that observation as a refutation, so a correct run read as a refutation on the second slide. (3) **The consequence model was wrong and its reachable second mode was missing.** Entries are assigned ABSOLUTELY from the anchor (`BuildEntries:742-749`) and `MigrateMasks` runs only when the anchor MOVED, so **no valid entry can keep its section across the call** — the draft's bucket (b) was satisfiable only by `SEC_VOID`↔`SEC_VOID`. The reachable mode is worse and appeared nowhere: a garbage id chance-matches a snapshot id, so a foreign NON-ZERO mask lands on an entry whose section is genuinely NEW and correctly zeroed, marking those entities already-loaded so they **NEVER SPAWN** — missing content, not duplicates, and probabilistic, so its absence refutes nothing. **The general shape: a probe designed to EXHIBIT a difference needs its non-vacuity control, its content precondition and its refutation list checked against each other, because an inverted-bar A/B has no "same = pass" fallback to catch a broken design.** Two smaller record defects rode along: the note told the executor to re-read the replay fixture's address from the OLD listing because "the fixture sits after the code that moved" (it did not move — the blob is at `$5E568` in BOTH debug ROMs and `$5C778` in both plain ROMs, which the parcel's own relocation-free headline already implied), and its RAM table put the DEBUG `Camera_X`/`Camera_Y` addresses in the PLAIN column. — RECORDED (bar: an inverted-bar A/B spec gets a lens panel before it is executed, not after).
- [migmask probe 2, 2026-08-06] **AN EMULATOR "RESUME" FROM A PC SITTING ON A BREAKPOINT RE-TRIGGERS IT WITHOUT EXECUTING — and the failure mode is a perfectly plausible, perfectly static negative result.** Cost the better part of a sitting: a scripted camera walk drove three pokes and a dozen resume/wait cycles while `Logic_Tick` stayed at **1** the entire time. Every poke landed in RAM, every read returned a coherent state, every `wait_for_break` returned the expected symbol — and not one frame had advanced. The observable is indistinguishable from "the drive ran and produced no difference", which is exactly the reading an inverted-bar A/B must never accept, because it has no "same = pass" fallback to catch a broken run. **The tell is a tick/frame counter that does not advance; the fix is `step 1` then `resume`.** It also fires at `EntryPoint` immediately after `reload_rom`. The overseer hit the same trap in the first sitting and recorded it — but recorded it as a probe-1 reading detail ("an identical register file on two consecutive breaks is the tell") rather than as a standing property of the instrument, so the next sitting paid for it again. That is the second-order lesson: **a trap written down inside one probe's prose does not generalise; it has to be filed against the INSTRUMENT.** — **RECORDED as a standing bar: every emulator A/B reads a tick/frame counter at two consecutive anchors and proves it advanced before reading anything else as evidence.**

- [mulw-parallax, 2026-08-07] **INSTRUMENT TRAP, new family member: the emulator's halt can land BEFORE the breakpoint address, while the log says the breakpoint triggered.** `wait_for_break` returned with the PC several instructions EARLIER than the armed address — `$006F16` for a breakpoint at `$006F22` (three instructions early; verified real by stepping, `$6F16` → `$6F1A`, so the reported PC was not a stale field), and `$0054BC` for `$0054C4`. It is intermittent: the same breakpoint halted exactly on address on later hits in the same run. **Why it is dangerous specifically for a behaviour-NEUTRAL A/B:** a reader who trusts the returned PC reads a register file from BEFORE the code under test, on both carts, and gets a clean match — an all-green result that measured nothing, which is indistinguishable from a correct pass. It is the sibling of the resume-on-a-breakpoint trap (which stalls the run) — that one freezes a counter, this one silently shifts the observation point. **BAR: after every `wait_for_break`, assert the PC equals the anchor and single-step forward until it does, before reading anything as evidence** — alongside the standing rule that a tick counter must be shown to advance across two consecutive anchors. — RECORDED

Two further hand-rolled runs of the same shape sit at `engine/level/plane_buffer.emp:325-344` and `:399`. A shared `pub comptime fn copy_run(src: Reg, dst: Reg, n: int) -> Code` in the `engine.coords` pure-comptime tier would serve all four; it needs register parameters, which `copy_band_entry` deliberately does NOT have (its call site hardcodes `a4` in the rebase, so a `dst` parameter would be a promise the call site cannot keep). So the hoist is a real parcel with a real design question, not a rename. — OPEN (kill: one shared generator lands with its own seven-target byte proof and `queue_static_dma` + both `plane_buffer` runs adopt it, or a ruling keeps them separate and `buffers.emp`'s ensure message is reworded to stop asking)
- [mulw-parallax panel, 2026-08-07] **The word cost edit can change WHICH candidate `choose()` picks, not merely how the winner is spelled — the packet's "no chain changes length" is a per-arm fact, not a pass-level one.** Making 5c cheaper moves it across the `mulu.w` boundary at some multipliers. Witness, priced from the same table the pass consumes: at `n = 3072 = 2^11 + 2^10` with a scratch, `mulu.w` costs 46 cy / 4 B; 5c cost 48 cy before the edit (so `mulu` won) and 44 cy after (so 5c wins) — **+6 ROM bytes and a scratch register that goes from untouched to written**. Not a soundness bug: both compute the same low word, the scratch is contract-licensed, and an undeclared scratch fails the build loudly because expansion precedes contract analysis. NO corpus multiplier is affected — the ten word multipliers are 10/26/66/66/80×4/160×2 — which is why every golden size is unmoved. It is recorded because the next adopter would otherwise read "no chain changes length" as a pass-level guarantee. `word_two_power_arm_is_dominated_by_ltr` cannot see this: it compares 5a-vs-5c deliberately, never against `choose()`'s winner. — OPEN (kill: an exhaustive `choose()`-vs-`mulu` boundary test lands, or a ruling accepts the per-arm phrasing)

The comments at the fill-mode selection, at `Parallax_Fill_PerCell`, and in `engine/ram.emp`'s shadow-band reservation all say "the config's own arrays when vshift = 0" or equivalent, describing an identity-copy shortcut that was never built or was removed. - [panel round, 2026-08-07] **NAMED DEFECT FAMILY: a test that pins the HAZARD as the contract.** Two instances now. (1) `mulw-parallax`: the copy-run test built an 11-field struct and asserted the derived run grows by one `move.b` — blessing, as proven-good, a size at which the run address-errors on every odd-indexed entry. (2) `2026-08-06`: a test pinned an INVERTED sentinel guard as the repo's only exemplar. Both tests were green, both were written by the parcel that introduced the hazard, and in both cases the test is what would have stopped the next reader noticing. The failure mode is specific: a negative-space test written from the implementation asserts what the code DOES, and the author reads its greenness as evidence the code is RIGHT. **SWEEP BAR for every future lane: each negative-space test a parcel adds gets one lens question — does this assert the CONTRACT, or the current BEHAVIOUR?** — RECORDED (bar, not a gap)
- [panel round, 2026-08-07] **A derived generator must state and check its ALIGNMENT precondition, not only its size arithmetic.** `copy_band_entry`'s pointer advance was provably correct for every size 0..16 — and that is exactly what made the odd case dangerous, because a correct advance with an odd stride alternates the parity of every array element, so a `move.l` run address-errors on every odd index. Size-correctness and alignment-correctness are independent properties and a generator that reasons about only the first will pass its own review. The rule generalises past this site: any comptime generator emitting width-typed accesses over an array derives TWO obligations from the struct — the element size and the element ALIGNMENT — and the second one is the one that crashes. — RECORDED (bar, not a gap)
- [panel round, 2026-08-07] **LANE C (`[layout.restated-size]`) is HELD, not abandoned — two design-level defects, with ratified redesign requirements.** (1) The check calls `layout_of_struct`, which `layout.rs` explicitly forbids in writing for exactly this shape ("the scan must NOT force `layout_of_struct` on the candidates — forcing layout runs each struct's declaration checks as a side effect"). A ZERO-FIRING warn-tier lint can therefore manufacture a hard error or move the frozen warn baseline, via a diagnostic that never names the lint. Inert on today's corpus by luck. (2) `SourceId` is not a sound identity for "declared in this file": `parse_str` stamps `SourceId(0)` on everything and ~96 port tests concatenate several separately-parsed modules into one synthetic file, so the `own_source` discriminator collapses across the whole port harness; the real build is safe only because `Manifest::scan` allocates distinct ids. A third lens found the discriminator has NO test — the shipped "scoping probe" exercises the ambient-slice path, not the prepended-into-`file.items` path the real build and every port test use. **REDESIGN REQUIREMENTS (Fable, ratified):** (a) the candidate scan uses a NON-FORCING size lookup — peek already-computed layouts, or run in a post-layout phase; it may never be the thing that forces layout; (b) own-file identity must survive `parse_str` and the concat harness — real `SourceId` allocation, or a discriminator that does not lean on it; (c) the prepended-into-`file.items` path gets an explicit test. The two aeon `#66` → `#sizeof(Sec)` adoptions RIDE the redesigned lane and are not to be split out — splitting makes `tile_cache.emp`'s `ensure(sizeof(Sec) == 66)` vacuous, and since the adoptions are byte-identical nothing is urgent. — OPEN (kill: the redesigned lint lands with all three requirements met)

- [closure-gate, 2026-08-07] **FOLLOW-UP LANE: the `Collision_Probe*` cluster — and a CORRECTION, because the first draft of this row described a mechanism `player_sensors.emp` does not have.** The draft (written from a relayed read, and mine to check) said the template has "TWO `rts` paths" whose early one is `moveq #16, d0 / rts` leaving `d1`/`d2` unwritten, and that a "both-miss tie (A = 16, B = 16)" returns a stale angle. **RE-VERIFIED AGAINST THE SOURCE, all three details are wrong.** `probe_core` has FOUR `rts`: `.done` is the ONLY proc-level return, and the other three are returns of the internal `.cell` subroutine reached by `jbsr .cell`. The `moveq #16, d0` at the top of the template is followed by `sub.w d3, d0` and FALLS INTO `.done` — it is not an exit. And the genuine-miss sentinel is **32, not 16**: `.nothing` is `moveq #32, d0 / moveq #0, d1 / moveq #0, d2 / jbra .done`, i.e. a real miss writes ALL THREE registers. So the both-miss case returns zeros, not garbage, and the premise the follow-up was going to trace does not hold as stated. **WHAT SURVIVES:** (i) the verifier hole is real and is still the thing to find first — `out(d0)` fires for all four probes although `d0` is written on every visible proc-level path, which smells like the `falls_into`/trailing-label family (5th instance if so), and it may be inflating the `d1`/`d2` rows. (ii) There IS a candidate stale path, but it is a different one: `.cl_hanging` ends `moveq #16, d0 / rts` as a `.cell` RETURN with `d1`/`d2` unwritten, and the caller then tests `cmpi.w #16, d0 / beq .full_back` where `.full_back` pushes `d1`/`d2` as the primary angle/attr. Whether that reaches SST state is the thing to trace. **The behavioural trace still comes before any taxonomy — it just has a different subject than the draft claimed.** — OPEN (kill: the `.cl_hanging` path is traced in the oracle and the cluster is classified on the result)

The demand site is real only if the trace above lands on "benign" or "contract-only" — if the fix is "the `#16` exits always write all three", the site vanishes and with it the entire demand. - [closure-gate panel, 2026-08-07] **THE UNSATISFIABLE-PIN WEDGE WAS A DEFECT IN THE SPECIFICATION, not in the build of it — recorded that way on the spec author's own ruling.** The parcel spec said "the build gate must consume the SAME baseline constants the CI tests assert against; two copies of a frozen baseline is how a pin forks". That is right about copies and silent about ANALYSES, and the second is what bites: the gate walks each shape under its own defines while the CI test walked DEFINE-FREE, and a define-free walk discards BOTH arms of every `if DEBUG == 1` / `if SOUND_DRIVER_ENABLED == 1`. It is a strict under-approximation of every shipped shape, not the view of any one of them — `contract_closure_corpus.rs`'s own header says exactly this, and the implementation carried a comment asserting the opposite. Consequence, one gated arm away from live: a firing gained inside a gated arm makes the pin UNSATISFIABLE — the define-free walk reports one count, the per-shape builds another, and no baseline value satisfies both, leaving only `CONTRACTS=0` or deleting a gate. **The general lesson is the one the sharing rule missed: a shared constant is only single-source if its CONSUMERS compute the same thing. Sharing an array between two different analyses is not deduplication, it is a hidden disagreement with one name.** Fixed by moving both baseline gates onto the per-shape walker, which also gave `d1c_baseline(true)` — the debug family's five rows — its first test coverage. — **CLOSED (fixed in the panel round; the three amendments to the spec's item 2 are ruled accepted)**

(2) A direct probe — `jbsr .helper / moveq #1,d0 / rts / .helper: … rts` — verifies **Produced**: the local body is unreachable in the CFG and is never charged. A second hypothesis (a narrowing write demoting an earlier full-width production) was also probed and refuted. **THE ACTUAL CAUSE, confirmed by probe and by the simplest failing corpus site:** the verifier's documented width rule credits production only from a FULL-WIDTH write — for a data register a `.l` or a `moveq`. `Collision_GetType` ends its main path `move.b (a0, d1.w), d0 / rts` and its air path `moveq #CTYPE_AIR, d0 / rts`; the byte path does not produce, so `out(d0)` fires. Widening that one write to `move.l` verifies — that is the whole discriminator, pinned in `tests/out_verify.rs::the_out_residue_is_a_width_gap_not_a_control_flow_one`, which also pins both refuted hypotheses so neither is re-proposed. **SURVEY of the flagged registers' write widths: `GetSineCosine` d0/d1 `.w` only, `Tile_Cache_GetTile` d2 `.w`, `Section_RedrawPlanes` d7 `.w`, `Emit_ObjectPieces` d5 `addq.b`, `EntityWindow_EntryForSection` d0 `moveq` + `.w`.** Every one produces a sub-width value and declares `out(rN)`, which means all 32 bits. **So the dominant class in the 30-row residue is BUCKET 3, a LANGUAGE-SURFACE GAP: `out` has no width facet, so the declaration cannot say what is true.** The address-register rows (`a1`/`a4` on `Art_Decompress`, `S4LZ_Decompress`, `S4LZ_DecompressDict`, `Load_Object`, `DrawRings`, `InsertSpriteMasks`) are a DIFFERENT and already-named cause — the write detection does not model `(An)+` advance (D2.35) — and they are also the three `[proc.out-unwritten]` per-file warnings, so one hole surfaces in both tiers. — OPEN (kill: a ruling on whether `out` gains a width facet — `out(d0.b)` / `out(d0.w)` — or the width rule is relaxed for a declared-narrow out; the taxonomy of the residue follows from that decision, and step 2's trace is unaffected either way)

- [collision lane fixup, 2026-08-07] **THE `falls_into` EXEMPTION DROPPED THE OUT OBLIGATION INSTEAD OF TRANSFERRING IT — an unsound widening, caught by all three lens agents independently and confirmed against the code before any fix.** As first landed, `out_verify`'s `Edge::FallOff` arm ran `check_return` only when `charge_fall_off_end`, so a proc declaring `falls_into Successor` had its fall-off charged to NOBODY: the successor's identity was never threaded in and nothing credited from it. `ok` is seeded all-true and only `check_return` clears it, so **a proc whose only exit is a fall-off ended the walk with every declared out `Produced` on zero evidence** — precisely the vacuity hole `preserves.rs:656-660` documents having closed ("a tail-only proc used to have ZERO obligation sites"). The polarity is the dangerous one the module header names: `verified_uncond_out` is consumed as a must-def DEFINITION by D1b (`corpus_contracts.rs`), an ERROR-tier gate with no baseline, so a claim wrongly blessed here silences a live check at every call site. **The corpus instance was live in the branch's own diff:** `S4LZ_DecompressDict out(a1) falls_into S4LZ_Decompress` (body `adda.w`/`suba.l`, `a1` only READ, no `rts`, no tail) was certified VERIFIED while `("S4LZ_Decompress","a1")` — the very claim it rests on — stayed in the baseline as UNVERIFIED, twelve lines apart in one array. **The precedent was misread:** `check_stack_balance` takes a `bool` and that IS sound for stack depth, because the successor's own unconditional balance check discharges the obligation; no such discharge exists for a value claim, since nothing requires a successor to declare the register at all. The right neighbour was `preserves`, which does not exempt — `ExitKind::FallOff => checkpoint_after_tail(st, Some(succ))` charges the successor's contract exactly as it charges a tail callee — and `out_verify`'s OWN `Edge::TailOut` arm was already the correct template. **Re-implemented:** `verify_out` now takes `falls_into: Option<&str>` (the successor NAME, not a bit — a bit cannot credit anything), and the `FallOff` arm credits the successor's VERIFIED uncond out and then charges `check_return`, monotone so the fixpoint argument is unaffected. `compute_verified_outs` takes a `BTreeMap<name, successor>`, which also collapses the two-sources-for-one-fact split the panel flagged. **Measured: residue back to 30**, `S4LZ_DecompressDict :: a1` restored as a CASCADE of `S4LZ_Decompress :: a1` — a third dependant of the same root, alongside `Art_Decompress`.

**WHERE THE DEFECT ENTERED, recorded because the record should show it: UPSTREAM OF THE PORTER, in the ruling.** The lane was directed to model the fix on `check_stack_balance`'s `charge_fall_off_end` precedent — the ruling prescribed the SIGNATURE, and the porter copied it faithfully. The signature was the wrong thing to transfer, and a bool cannot express "the obligation moves". Fable has ratified the correction as a **standing bar for BOTH sides of the relay: a cited precedent transfers only together with its soundness argument.** Here that argument is one sentence and it is decisive — *stack balance may DROP the charge because the successor's own unconditional balance check discharges it; no value claim has that discharge.* Had the precedent been cited with it, the mismatch would have been visible before a line was written. A precedent quoted by name alone is an unexamined premise wearing a citation. — **CLOSED (re-implemented as a transfer; the row it "removed" is back where it belongs)**
- [collision lane fixup, 2026-08-07] **A negative test that pinned the vacuity as the contract — the named defect family, in the parcel that had just been warned about it.** `a_falls_into_procs_fall_off_end_is_not_a_required_return` asserted `is_produced` while passing `verify_out` an EMPTY callee map and no successor name. It could not have checked that anything produces `a1`; delete the successor from the program entirely and it still passed. Its doc claimed "both polarities are pinned" but the polarities varied the FLAG, not the SUCCESSOR — so it would keep passing under an implementation that ignores the successor altogether, which is exactly the implementation it was shipped with. The repo already owned the correct shape one file away (`tests/preserves.rs`'s four `empty_body_falls_into_{preserving,clobbering}_successor` cases, which vary the successor's contract and get opposite verdicts). Replaced with `a_falls_into_procs_fall_off_charges_the_successor`, four cases: successor produces → verifies; **successor does NOT produce → FIRES** (the case a blanket exemption cannot express, and the one that proves the successor is consulted); unknown/extern successor credits nothing → fires; no successor → fires. **THIRD INSTANCE of the test-pins-the-hazard family**, after the lane-A panel's inverted-sentinel guard (pinned as the repo's only exemplar) and the derived-generator test that blessed a crashing size as proven-good. The count matters because the family's signature is now legible: each instance was a test whose ASSERTION was true of the code as written and false of the contract, and in each case the tell was that the test could not fail for the reason it named. Asking "does this assert the CONTRACT or the current BEHAVIOUR?" of every negative test is the bar; the sharper operational form is **"name the mutant this test is supposed to catch, then run it"** — which is what caught both halves here and, one round later, the plumbing gap. — **CLOSED**

Every later measurement — fresh builds in the lane worktree, the main checkout re-run, and the frozen `OUT_UNVERIFIED_BASELINE` in source — agrees master is **30 with that row present**. Cargo reported "Finished in 0.02s", i.e. it never rebuilt. No load-bearing conclusion rested on it (all of those were derived in the lane worktree from fresh builds or read from source), but it cost real time to chase. **Two bars: prefix EVERY command with an explicit `cd` to the lane worktree, and treat a sub-second "Finished" on a supposedly-fresh measurement as a staleness signal, not as speed.** Ratified sharper: **a suspiciously fast gate is a PROVENANCE question before it is a result** — ask which tree and which binary produced it before reading the number at all.
